# Tasks — Unity migration

**Latest scene authoring, 2026-09-15:** World.unity now exposes editable buildings, props, resources, roads, paved areas and terrain overrides, compiled into the simulation at session start. [Editing guide and verification](SCENE_EDITING_2026-09-15.md). C-ACC remains human.

**Latest ore opening, 2026-09-15:** approved ore-to-plate recipes, power-independent Home smelting and eastern-yard deposits are implemented for New Game. Older saves retain their original layout/balance. [Behavior and verification](ORE_OPENING_2026-09-15.md). C-ACC remains human.

**Latest inventory correction, 2026-09-15:** visible empty fuel/input/output/storage slots, pointer-following drag preview, validated drops, hotbar structure drops and inventories-first small-screen layout. [Behaviour and actual gesture checks](INVENTORY_FIXES_2026-09-15.md). C-ACC remains human.

**Latest activity feedback, 2026-09-15:** live cable highlights, machine working/full/no-power cues, generator running/standby/off and conveyor movement/jam animation are implemented. Conveyors remain independent of electricity by owner choice. [Behaviour and evidence](ACTIVITY_ANIMATIONS_2026-09-15.md).

**Latest debug tools, 2026-09-15:** F8 / Admin opens resource and weapon grants, enemy and raid controls, player/base recovery, pause and temporary testing overrides. [Controls and verified behaviour](ADMIN_MENU_2026-09-15.md). C-ACC remains human.

**Latest gameplay correction, 2026-09-15:** power state in hover/inspection, visible extractor output, smooth spaced conveyor items, single direction arrows, persistent power cables and pole previews are implemented. [Changes and actual Unity verification](FLOW_POWER_FIXES_2026-09-15.md). C-ACC remains human.

**Current owner-authorised illustrated UI pass, 2026-09-15:** the approved reference style is implemented with reusable painted frames, fourteen painted item icons, compact HUD/Backpack, live recipe equations and target-adjacent feedback. [Implementation, assets and actual Unity captures](UI_INDUSTRIAL_PASS_2026-09-15.md). Earlier C-UX controls and gameplay remain intact. **C-ACC remains human; world art and standalone/campaign acceptance remain open.**

**This file is the only owner of migration task status.** The reference tracker `docs/PROGRESS.md` is paused and points here. One log entry per meaningful status change goes in §8.

Status values: `todo` · `in progress` · `done` · `blocked (reason)` · `human` (needs an owner verdict, never marked done by an agent). Code status, automated checks, design approval and human play are recorded separately; a passing test never closes a `human` row.

Phase rule: a phase is complete only when every non-human task in it is `done` and its playable acceptance has been run by a person and recorded. Later phases may start engineering work early only where a task's dependencies are all `done`.

Sources for every task are in [MIGRATION_MAP.md](MIGRATION_MAP.md) (system entries `S-nn`, `S-Ann`) and the specialist documents; a task cites the entries it implements rather than repeating source paths. Decisions and open questions (`U-D`, `U-M`, `U-P`, `U-Q`) are in [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md).

## Phase B — Unity foundation, data and simulation core

Goal (revised 2026-09-11, U-M-22): a working Unity project that compiles cleanly with its editor tooling verified; an engine-independent simulation foundation (state, tick, commands and events, PRNG, inventory and ledger, map and movement core, save/load, timing host) with a few small checks (inventory conservation, save/load round trip, sim timing, replay consistency); the data assets the foundation and the opening need; and one visible scene with movement, one machine and an editable UI Toolkit panel. No authored world, no production chain and no complete gameplay yet: crafting and machines (B-07), power (B-09) and belts (B-10) moved into Phase C, where the opening needs them, with their IDs kept. Verification is proportionate: each subsystem's checks live on the task that ports it; broader harnesses, bots, nightly runs and soaks are deferred with explicit triggers (DEF-01–DEF-04 below, U-M-23). Exit: B-ACC passes.

Scope rule for Phase B (2026-09-11): checks are written for behaviour that exists; nothing is tested before it is built. A ported reference test that fails in Unity is investigated when it is ported, not triaged in bulk. The 146 reference test failures (318/464 on 2026-09-11) are **reported failures whose individual provenance is not established**; they are neither labelled pre-existing wholesale nor made a migration prerequisite (MIGRATION_MAP.md §1).

Intentional differences: (1) `maxTicks` is counted in tile ticks (`MaxTicksPerCall` = 5,120 = 256 s) not seconds; (2) `T` advances 1/20 per tick continuously (the reference only bumped it per second); (3) the accumulator lives on `Simulation`, not in saved state, and there is no `speed`; (4) `hourRow`/block economy of `step` omitted (retired, §17); (5) per-second `shots`/`iframes` resets deferred to Phase C combat; (6) `ITileLayer.AfterCommand` fires only for accepted commands (documented defect correction, the reference also fired on refusals); (7) `SecondPhase` runs at the top of tick n+1 rather than after tick n, same observable order; (8) doubles serialise shortest-round-trip (U-M-13); (9) `Prng.PickIndex` is a new helper; (10) danger counters live on `Engineer` (reference `player.danger`), moved by the coordinator from `SimState`. A new game reads its starting stake from `GameData.Stake` (`InventoryInitializer`), and `InventoryInitializer.StakeProblem` reports a missing or invalid stake asset. `Relight.Presentation/Persistence/AutosaveController.cs` (the only engine-facing file: `persistentDataPath`, `SystemFileSystem`, `SimHost.TickBoundary`, save-on-quit, `Debug.LogWarning` on failure); tests `Tests/Sim/Persistence/{PersistenceTestSupport (MemoryFileSystem with injectable faults), SaveRoundTripTests 7, SaveRefusalTests 17, AutosaveTests 20}.cs`. Schema v1, kind `relight-save-unity`, one canonical JSON document, header `{schema, kind, dataVersion, savedAt, playSeconds, hash}` with the checksum covering only `state`; `playSeconds` is an unhashed header field on a `partial SimState` (U-D-41); transients excluded by not being visited; every write is `.tmp` → durable write → atomic replace with a retained `.bak`; layout `{persistentDataPath}/saves/exploration-v2/slot-<name>.json`, `auto/auto-<i>.json`, `auto-quit.json`, `auto-index.json` (TA §9.4.1); recorded defaults (interval, ring size, event and quit saves) in `AutosaveSettings`; 3-strike failure policy with resume on manual save. **Sources:** `packages/sim/src/save.ts:1–120` (envelope, `SAVE_TRANSIENT` :48, hash over state), `saveLoad.ts` (refusal order version → kind → shape), `packages/game/src/session.ts:~190–260` (save/load wiring, save on quit; confirms no reference autosave), TA §9.1–9.4.6/§10.4–10.6, U-D-04/27/32/35/41, S-05/S-37/U-M-06/U-M-13. **Intentional differences and corrected defects:** different marker (`relight-save-unity`, not interchangeable with reference saves); no migrations and no converter (exactly v1, everything else refused by number); checksum mandatory (reference advisory); `dataVersion` mismatch warns and loads (never refuses); transient exclusion by construction rather than by name; atomic writes (reference wrote in place); a corrupt own file is set aside as `.corrupt` and the loader falls back to `.bak` then the next-newest ring slot (reference deleted/overwrote); foreign files are refused and left untouched, never renamed; the whole autosave ring, index and `playSeconds` are new (no reference). **Checks:** scratch `dotnet test` 44 persistence tests green; Unity EditMode on `Unity/Relight`: 172 total, 171 passed, 0 failed (`evidence/phase-b/b12-editmode-wave2-2026-09-12.xml`) — the first Unity run had 20 persistence failures caused by `MemoryFileSystem` keying directories with `/` while `Path.GetDirectoryName` returns `\` on Windows; fixed by normalising in the test support (`Dir()`), production untouched; H1 wired in `SharedChecksTests.SaveThenLoadGivesTheSameHash` (hash, canonical text and conservation equal after write → read of the shared short run); `AutosaveController.cs` compiled by the Unity EditMode and PlayMode runs (0 `error CS`). **Limitations:** the real `SystemFileSystem` is not exercised by a test (all tests use the in-memory file system; the `File.Replace` → `File.Move` fallback and `PlatformNotSupportedException` branch are reasoned, not observed — the first real save in the editor is a B-ACC observation); no directory fsync (accepted, TA §9.4.4); the TA §9.4.3 background writer is not implemented (write is synchronous behind `IFileSystem`; Phase F measures the cost); event triggers exist (`AutosaveScheduler.Trigger`) but nothing calls them yet (Phase C rows own the events); settings persistence and the HUD notice surface are not built (notice string exists on the scheduler); save-file size 1,747 bytes for the round-trip state (one chest, 57 ticks); **handoff closure 2026-09-12:** one real filesystem round trip performed by the `[Explicit]` `Relight.Tests.Play.DiskRoundTrip` pair (`Tests/Play/Persistence/DiskRoundTripTests.cs`) in two Unity processes — phase 1 moves the engineer (6.5, 6.5)→(9.5, 8.5), places a chest at (10, 8), transfers 7 steel into it and saves slot `roundtrip-check` through `SystemFileSystem`; ---

## Phase C — Complete Home opening, including saves

Goal: the opening is fully playable and correct in Unity on the real Founders Court area, giving the intended experience "I built a turret, saw it protect me, automated its supply, and could then leave to explore": New Game → starting stake → mining → Generator and power → first fully loaded operational turret → short directional warning → small introductory attack from one approach → visible ammunition consumption → automatic replenishment through real delivery → three turrets covering different approaches → a useful nearby excursion while production continues, with save/continue at any point. Focused, documented tuning of provisional values is permitted in this phase (U-M-12). Exit: C-ACC passes.

## Correction pass — owner playtest of the Home opening (2026-09-14)

Owner brief after playing the Phase C build (screenshot `5.png`): poor UI presentation, no tooltips, only a crop of the level, a click on the ground walked the engineer, Tab did not open the Backpack and B did nothing. Authorised scope: a focused correction pass — controls/UI fixes plus the full riverfront map import and presentation brought forward from Phase D; later campaign content stays in its phases. Executed by the coordinator (Fable) with three Opus workers under exclusive file ownership (W-A controls/shell, W-B UI/build/tooltips, W-C full map/saves/presentation); the coordinator alone drove Unity. Rulings R-CP-1–12 and the findings are DECISIONS.md U-M-39 and U-M-40. Evidence: [`evidence/correction-pass/`](evidence/correction-pass/) — `repro/repro-hud.png`, `repro/repro-tab.png` (the reproduced defects), `full-map-import.md` (export/import counts and hashes) and the seven final captures listed under CP-07. Suites after the pass: **EditMode 563/563 passed**, **PlayMode 60 run / 55 passed / 0 failed / 5 skipped** (the 50 s timing form, three R7-Tilemap prototypes by design, and one drag test whose fixture save carries no split stack). No git commit was made (owner did not ask).

## 6. Dependency summary

- Phase B is a foundation, not a feature phase: A → B-01 → B-02 → B-02a (editor tooling) and {B-03 → B-04, B-08, B-12}; B-05 after B-02; B-06 after B-03 and B-05; B-11 after B-06; B-13 after B-04/B-05/B-08; B-14 after B-13 and before C-06/C-11/D-09 fix scene structure. DEF-01–DEF-04 are trigger-gated with no dependants.
- B-07, B-09 and B-10 (crafting and machines, power, belts) sit in Phase C with their IDs kept: B-07 after B-06, B-09 after B-07 and B-08, B-10 after B-09; C-04 needs B-09, C-06 needs B-07, C-07 needs B-09, D-05 needs B-09, D-06 needs B-10. The sim-side light model is in C-11.
- C needs B-ACC plus the minimum pipeline D-01a → D-02a for C-01; no other Phase D task is a Phase C prerequisite. (The B-ACC half of that sentence no longer holds in practice: on 2026-09-14 the owner deferred the manual B-ACC checks as a prerequisite for starting Phase C and authorised the phase, as §8 records. B-ACC itself is still `human` and still owed.) D-01a/D-02a are implemented inside Phase C limited to the Home region (the only later-phase dependency the opening needs). C-08 needs B-08 and C-04; C-09 is the phase's integrating task; C-10 originates the front end the reference never had.
- D-01a/D-01b are reference-side tooling and can start as soon as A-08 is done; they are the only planned change to the reference project. D-02b and everything that needs the whole city (D-03, D-04, D-07, D-08, D-09) follow D-01b.
- E-09–E-15 and F-01 no longer wait on any approval gate: the owner approved the full gameplay scope (U-D-31), so each depends only on its engineering prerequisites, and their values are provisional and tunable (U-M-12, U-D-28). E-16 follows E-15 and carries the later raid tiers. F-07 follows E-16 alone: performance is certified against the art actually in the build, because the pending art package must not block a playable milestone (U-D-29).
- Only two rows in the whole plan wait on the owner. F-03 waits on the art package and the tileset licence (U-Q-04, external information), and F-05's implementation rows F-05a–c wait on the ending (U-Q-02) while F-05's plateau handling proceeds now. Nothing in Phases B–E depends on either. F-ACC treats both as conditional in the same way: it is accepted on placeholder art with the art state named if the package has not arrived, and F-05a–c join it only once the ending is answered. F-06 is closed as `done (not required)` (U-D-27) and F-ACC does not list it. **Update 2026-09-20:** U-D-60 answered the ending, so only F-03 now waits on the owner, and F-05a–c are open as todo and are F-ACC dependencies.
- Audio is in scope (U-D-33): C-11 defines the `AudioCue` hook, D-11 builds the cue catalogue, mixer and first clip set, C-10 exposes the volume controls, and F-04 completes ambience and the mix. D-ACC and F-ACC include them; no audio row is optional any more.
- Nothing gates Phase B except the owner's go-ahead: the baseline is the preserved snapshot (U-M-19, confirmed by U-D-36), the Unity target is fixed (U-M-01, confirmed by U-D-30), and B-11 proceeds on settled owner decisions — fresh start with no converter (U-D-27), an inert `profile` field (U-D-32), manual slots plus a rotating autosave ring (U-D-35).
- **Mandated order inside the threat work (added 2026-09-21; an order, not a dependency).** U-D-66 fixes the build order as: the failure path (E-18), then the tools (E-19), then the feedback (E-20), then the curve and fair timing (E-21), then roamer behaviour (L-03), then alien roles and raid types (E-13, E-14, E-22). U-D-64 (d) puts the failure path first, and U-D-62 builds L-02, E-13, E-14 and E-17 ahead of E-11 and E-12. None of these changes a Dependencies cell above. Linear records them as labelled order lines, not as blockers. One conflict is open: L-03 depends on E-13 here, which is the reverse of U-D-66 (REL-71 F1-33).
- **Feasibility checkpoint (added 2026-09-21).** FEAS-01, in the backlog reconciliation section below, is a paper check that comes before the large Phase D and E rows are built. It changes no Dependencies cell; Linear holds it as a labelled checkpoint in front of D-05/D-06, E-02, E-03, E-05, F-05a and GP-W8.

## 7. Known residue after Phase A

- The provisional values (U-P-01–U-P-09) are ported as starting points with their chronology in DECISIONS.md §3. Focused, documented tuning during Unity playtesting is delegated to the workers (U-D-28, U-M-12); the owner is not asked to approve individual numbers, and F-02 is the record rather than a gate. Two values were set by the owner rather than taken from code: Breaker 220 (U-P-08) and the guardian trio 600 / 1,000 / 1,500 (U-P-09).
- Unity 6.6 is the fixed target (U-D-30) and `6000.6.0f1` was observed installed on 2026-09-11; B-01 records the exact version, the package manifest and the two shell deviations; the MCP integration's real capabilities are unverified until B-02a; UI Toolkit drag-and-drop, `Tilemap` scale and the lighting approach are unverified until B-14.
- Old-save import is not required and not built (U-D-27): the Unity build starts fresh and the reference saves stay with the reference game. F-06 is closed.
- The reference checkpoint is uncommitted; the baseline is the preserved snapshot `reference-snapshot.sha256` (U-M-19). No commit is made without authorisation.
- The 146 reference sim test failures are reported failures with unestablished individual provenance: a sample was inspected and looked pre-existing (`docs/Implementation/GP_CHECKPOINT.md:178`), and no more than that is claimed. They are neither triaged in bulk (that is DEF-01, trigger-gated) nor inherited as expectations: a ported test is written against the intended behaviour in GAME_DESIGN.md and DECISIONS.md, never ported as a passing expectation because it currently fails, and never assumed correct because it currently passes (Phase B scope rule).
- The reference's human gates were never passed; F-08 replaces them with plain-language owner play checks (old IDs kept as provenance). The two browser retests on port 5178 are not repeated (U-D-43): the intended fixes are carried into Unity and verified there as C-12 checks, and the browser evidence is recorded as superseded, never as passed.
- The `Assets/urban-decay/48px+MVMZ` tileset licence is unknown and the owner's art package is pending (U-Q-04, U-D-29); Unity must not reference either until source and licence arrive, and neither may block foundation work or a playable milestone. F-03 is the only affected row.
- Roster naming: the Unity roster is the five regular types Skitter, Spitter, Stalker, Breaker and Howler, with guardians separate (U-D-37). The legacy third enemy type was **Hulk**, not Conductor; Conductor is a campaign role.
- Telemetry is retired (U-D-34, DEF-03) and gamepad support is out of initial scope (U-D-42, DEF-04); both are owner decisions and neither blocks anything.

## Gameplay improvement pass — owner brief 2026-09-15

The owner authorised one implementation pass over the playable opening and defence loop: *"Relight should be demanding, understandable and rewarding to master. Poor preparation should cause shortages, damaged defences, failed expeditions and meaningful setbacks. Good preparation, factory design and combat skill should noticeably improve the outcome. Regular raids consume supplies and expose weaknesses. Major assaults test sustained production and defence. Exploration unlocks useful capabilities and advances city restoration."* The existing plan for the complete game is preserved; approved dependencies are brought forward only where an item needs them.

**Owner acceptance is pending for every row below. Nothing here has been playtested or accepted by the owner.**

| ID | Scope | Depends on | Acceptance | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GP-W1 | **Unattended Home workshop (U-D-44).** One persistent workshop queue with stable job ids; ingredients reserved out of the Backpack at queue time so they cannot be spent twice; a physical output tray collected in reach, never delivered remotely; a full tray pauses the job at full progress without consuming or repeating; cancellation refunds every unprocessed ingredient; jobs, reservations, tray and progress survive save/load; existing saves and legacy craft queues migrate without duplication or loss; the UI Toolkit workshop panel shows ingredients, quantities, duration, queue, progress, tray and clear Cancel/Collect controls and says that production continues while away. Recipe costs, durations and power requirements are unchanged. | B-07, C-05, C-06 | Conservation, cancellation, full-tray, migration and save/resume covered by focused sim tests (592 pass); the movement lock narrowed to a core repair and pinned by test | done — implementation and focused checks, 2026-09-15; owner acceptance pending |
| GP-W2 | **Decision record and guidance amendments.** U-D-44 recorded; U-D-10 marked superseded with what survives and what is withdrawn; UI_AND_ONBOARDING.md §4/§6/§9, CONTENT_CATALOGUE.md and GAME_DESIGN.md amended; B-07 and C-06 annotated. | GP-W1 | No document still asserts the immobilising craft as current behaviour | done 2026-09-15 |
| GP-W3 | **Raid scheduling, warnings and placement feedback.** Major-assault staging computed from the actual map, reachable approaches and valid spawn locations, with no invalid fallback accepted; a configurable minor-raid preparation warning (initially 30–45 s) with an accurate approach indication; stale notices cleared; announced information matches the encounter that arrives; approaches chosen before they are announced and varied where the map supports it; encounter state preserved across save/load so warnings and waves cannot duplicate or disappear; the first-turret teaching encounter kept separate from later sieges and fired once from a loaded, operational turret; turret range and obstructed sight shown during placement with a clear blind-position warning, and the tutorial's suggested position made viable. **Delivered (U-D-45, U-D-46):** minor raids split into a warning and a wave with `SiegeTuning` 30 + 0..15 s of preparation; one shared reachability/clearance definition behind `Origin`, `Approaches` and `Staging`; authored camps joining rather than replacing the map's approaches; every notice given an expiry; `SaveSchema` 6 → 7 with a v6 wave upgraded rather than re-announced; `Sightline.Clear` made the single line-of-fire rule for turrets, enemy target selection and the engineer's rifle; `TurretSight.Coverage` drawn as the placement outline with a blind/restricted advisory. | GP-W1 | Focused encounter-scheduling, notice-expiry, save/resume and v6→v7 migration tests (`DirectorTests`, `DirectorSaveTests`); staged origins and headings verified against the shipped Home export through a scratch console harness; turret coverage and the wall rule pinned by `TurretSightTests` | done — implementation and focused checks, 2026-09-15; owner acceptance pending |

Three to five waves over six to eight minutes is an initial tuning proposal, not a required result. Values and reasoning recorded in **U-D-47**; tunables on `Data/Generated/Tuning/Tuning - Siege.asset` (`SiegeTuningAsset`), with `SiegeTuning.Fallback` until the owner runs the Unity data generator | done — implementation and focused checks, 2026-09-15; owner acceptance pending |

## Always dark — owner decision 2026-09-19 (U-D-58)

The owner decided the world is always dark, with no day and night cycle, and approved [ALWAYS_DARK_SPEC.md](ALWAYS_DARK_SPEC.md), which owns the rules. **Owner acceptance is pending for every row below.**

The operating-state enum C-07 reads is `MachineOperatingState { Idle, Running, Throttled, Unpowered, OutOfFuel, OutputFull, NoInput }`. **No outage at start** (U-D-12) and a `NetworkKey` struct replaces the reference's string circuit key (S-25). 27 new tests; the run stood at 288 total / 279 passed, the 9 failures being pre-existing Wave-0 tests drifting against W-C's new placement cost. **W-C (C-02, C-03):** `Sim/Actor/Engineer.Equipment.cs`, `Sim/Actor/Mining/{Mining,MiningCommands}.cs`, `Sim/Building/Placement.cs` (charges `MachineSpec.Cost`, refunds the machine on removal, splits `Validity` from `Buildable`, gates on the hand lock) and `Sim/Combat/Weapons/{WeaponState,Ballistics,Equipment,WeaponPhase,WeaponQueries,SimComposition.Weapons}.cs`, ported from `flow.ts:1019,1333-1340,1367-1375,1439-1457`, `worldScene.ts:215,704,711,728,743,810`, `equipment.ts`, `weaponProfiles.ts:13` and `playerBallistics.ts:9-14`; Unity-side `Presentation/WorldInput.cs`, `EngineerView.cs`, `Presentation/Engineer/MiningFeedbackPresenter.cs`, `Presentation/Combat/TracerPresenter.cs` and `Input/RelightControls.inputactions` (`World/Equip` E, `Global/Reload` R, `Global/Slot` 1-9 and 0) were written but not compiled by the worker. 31 new tests. W-C diagnosed the 9 drifted tests and supplied the exact patches (`Tests/Sim/Actor/InventoryFixture.cs` funds the cost, `Tests/Sim/Support/Scenarios.cs` 3 batches / 30 bullets / 4-in-1-out, `ReplayConsistencyTests`, `SharedChecksTests` `Sinks[Steel]` 10 → 16), which the coordinator applied. **Rulings recorded this wave:** removal refunds the machine, not its price; `HandCraft.HandLocked` is not extended to the rifle craft; weapons cannot be stored in chests (deferred beyond Phase C); a processor's output goes to `Machine.Inv[outputItem]`; the 24 worker-authored `.meta` files are kept for Phase C, superseding `contracts.md`'s "no .meta" rule.

- 2026-09-14 — **Phase C Wave 4 verification (coordinator Fable, Unity 6000.6.0f1 with the owner's editor open, driven through the Unity CLI 1.0.0-beta.8).** **EditMode: 527 / 527 passed** (`Relight.Sim.Tests`) at the start of the wave, and the Unity-free `dotnet test` sim loop agreed at 527/527; the final EditMode run after the integrated fixes below is **543 / 543** (6.09 s), including the new `OpeningSupplyTests` (2), `HomeDepotTests` (4) and `PlacementSolidTests` (1). **PlayMode full suite:** first run 41 total / 28 passed / 9 failed / 4 skipped — 3 of the skips are the R2/R7 prototype scene absent by design and 1 is a long timing form; after the fixes below a run reported 41 total / 32 passed / 5 failed; the final rerun result is 44 run / 40 passed / 0 failed / 4 skipped (07:00 UTC 2026-09-14; the skips are the 50 s timing form and three R7-Tilemap tests, by design; the `DiskRoundTrip` Phase1/2 and `EvidenceCapture` forms are `[Explicit]` and were not run). The Phase B Play tests were adapted where Phase C changed the surface: `AutosaveSessionTests.NearlyDue`, the `WorldSceneTests` walk-here check, the `SessionChangeTests`/`WorldSceneTests` placements, and the U6 card greying its button. **Four production fixes were made during verification, in the shipped code:** `UI/Hud/Hud.uss` `#status-strip` was stretching and is now content-width (defect U-5); `UI/Guide/GoalCard.uss` takes a 96 px top offset because the goal card overlapped the status panel; `Presentation/CameraRig.cs` re-snaps to the engineer when `SimHost.Session` changes; and `UI/StatusPanelController.cs` walk-here is now a relative six-tile step (`walkStepTiles`), which orphans the old absolute `walkTarget` scene value (harmless, but it should be removed when the scene is next edited). An earlier PlayMode rerun was aborted by the editor ("Playmode tests were aborted because the player was stopped") and restarted.  **C-01–C-12, B-07, B-09 and B-10 are `done` on this evidence, several with recorded limitations** (see the Phase C table); C-ACC stays `human` and unrun, and B-ACC stays `human`, deferred by the owner rather than passed. **Interface and production fixes from the same pass:** `WorkshopPanelController` now force-paints on the first frame the workshop is shown, so the drawer can no longer open on a stale core card (the 150 ms throttle alone let `OpeningUiPlayTests.U6` see a disabled repair button; the reference does `adapter.enter()` before `sync()`, `uiShell.ts:63-70`); the status panel became a right-side drawer at `top: 96px`, closed at start (`UiShell.tabPanel`, scene `GameUI.unity` saved); `StatusPanelController` scopes its queries to `#status-panel` because the HUD's own `clock` label was being found first and froze the panel clock at 00:00; `.notice-row` and `.alert-strip` take `max-width: 100%` so a long director notice wraps; and `#objective-text` gained a translucent plate because accent text over the tan Depot sprite was unreadable. All verified live at 1600x900 and 960x540 (`fix-*.png`). **The integrated Play Mode run** was a fresh game on the imported region: the chain built, the Rifle crafted and equipped, a turret placed and loaded, the opening encounter scheduled, `slot-phasec-int` saved and loaded back over the active attack with no duplicate wave, the MainMenu -> New Game flow verified, and the three-turret step and automatic resupply verified after the `Machine.Out` fix, with two debug raids. Captures and the two saves' provenance are in [`evidence/phase-c/integrated-run/`](evidence/phase-c/integrated-run/) — `raid2-1600x900.png`, `raid2-960x540.png` and six `fix-*.png` interface captures; the saves `slot-phasec-int.json` and `slot-phasec-int-end.json` are copied beside them (originals in the owner's `saves/exploration-v2/` folder). **Root cause found in the run:** automatic resupply never registered because `Machine.Out` is never written and the opening read that retired field; fixed in `OpeningRules.OutputBuffer`/`ProducedThisTick` and `OpeningPhase.NoteProduction` with buffered `OpeningQueries`, **no save-schema change** (the buffer is derivable), covered by `OpeningSupplyTests` (2). Two further rulings from the run: `Placement.GeometryProblem` refuses a solid city tile ("a city structure is there") per the reference (`PlacementSolidTests`, 1); and `HomeCoreInitializer` places one 6x6 Depot inside the authored core at new game (`Sim/Campaign/HomeCore/HomeDepot.cs`), while `HandCraft.NearDepot` is also true within reach of the authored Home rect, so a save made before the Depot existed still crafts at Home (`HomeDepotTests`, 4). The Play scene tests were made map-agnostic for the imported region (`SceneFixture.FreeTile`/`Last`; the chest view is checked by id because the Depot draws through the `PrefabRegistry` fallback, the Chest placeholder). **Owner-facing findings, not fixed:** a turret placed inside the workshop walls cannot see out and never fires, and the introductory attack then disables the core (observed in the run; the fix candidates are an objective hint to place it outside the walls, or sight through Home walls); skitters down a close-range engineer in about 10 s; after a repair the objective chain can re-surface an earlier step's problem text ("Start your extraction line - output full") when the chest output is full or the generator is empty; the debug raid needs `st.Director.DebugAllowed` and refuses an origin within 28 tiles of the engineer; and the run used debug material grants (120 Steel / 60 Copper / 10 Coal, then 100 Steel / 40 Copper, then +30 Copper) which are not player-facing. **Verification limitation:** PlayMode `run_tests --filter` returns 0 tests through the CLI, so only the unfiltered PlayMode run (about 25 s) is usable; EditMode filters need the full fixture name. **No git commits were made during Phase C** (coordinator ruling): the whole tree is uncommitted, including `Unity/Relight/Packages/manifest.json` and `packages-lock.json`, the untracked `.claude/skills/`, the untracked `Unity/Relight/Assets/Relight/Sim/UI/Hud/`, and a git-ignored leftover `Unity/Relight/Assets/InitTestScene….unity` from the test runner (safe to delete). The owner handoff for this phase is **[PHASE_C_HANDOFF.md](PHASE_C_HANDOFF.md)**.

- **2026-09-15 — GP-W5 done (engineering; owner acceptance pending):** combat and losses were connected to the configured data and made recoverable (**U-D-48**). The invulnerable barrier was fixed at its SOURCE: `Sim/Data/CombatBalance.cs`, a new port-owned layer applied after `OpeningBalance` in both data paths, gives every buildable machine an integrity row (`min(120, 20 + 2 × costItems)`) — so `MachineSpec.Hp` is no longer 0 for everything but wall/barricade/turret/cannon, `TurretRules.IsDefence` is no longer false for a Supply chest, and `DirectorRules.HostileOpen` and `RaidField.Open` became correct with **no edit of their own**. Walk-through kinds (belt, fast belt, pole, track, tram) deliberately get NO integrity, so a raid can never stall itself chewing the cheapest thing on the map. The same layer brings the approved Breaker forward from CONTENT_CATALOGUE §7.4 (220 hp, 15 damage, 2 s interval, 2.7 tiles/s) — a roster addition, not an invention. Twelve hard-coded values in `EnemyPhase` became table reads: perception 1.2 → `raids.NoticeTiles` (8), contact and structure damage through one rule `def.Damage × StructureMul` where `StructureMul = StructureDps / ContactDps = 1.6` (which reproduces the exported skitter 8 dps exactly and gives the Breaker §7.2's ×3 per bite without a second mechanic), chase speed `def.SpeedTilesPerS`, march speed scaled off the skitter baseline, and contact reach `def.RangeTiles`. `TickCombatActor` gained a readable leash: a camp resident commits until IT is `GuardPursuitTiles` (36) from its OWN camp, breaks off **still remembering the engineer**, walks home, and turns round only inside `GuardReengageTiles` (22) — replacing a circle drawn round the camp, measured against where the player stood, that wiped the body's memory mid-stride and made retreating free. `SiegePlan` gained `WaveBreakers`: none in the first assault, then 5% of the roster from the second assault's third wave, taken OUT of the skitters so the announced head count still arrives, and placed at the FRONT of each carrying wave because a Breaker marches at 1.0 tiles/s against a skitter's 2.0. For the second assault's 69 bodies that is waves of **10 / 15 / 19 / 25** carrying **0 / 6 / 7 / 10** spitters and **0 / 0 / 1 / 2** Breakers — ~267 turret rounds against the first assault's 207. A wreck now stays on the map at 0 hp and stops working EVERYWHERE at once — power throttle and network, outage reporting, flow routing, operating state, the shared walk grid and line of sight all read the same ruin — and `HomeQueries.MachineRepairCard`/`MachineRepairLine` with `HomeCore.RepairProblem` finally SHOW that it is repairable, at the machine, quoting the price (2 Steel + 1 Copper restores 40 hp in 4 s), how many instalments a full repair takes and the sim's own refusal for a greyed button. `Sim/Actor/DeathCache.cs` drops the Backpack contents into a pile on the tile the engineer fell on which never despawns and is never culled (a backpack can hold an Alien Key, a schematic or an artifact — progression must not be destroyed by a death); weapon keys are retained; two deaths on one tile merge into one pile; `Ledger` counts the pile where it stands, so nothing is a source and nothing is a sink. `SaveSchema.Version` **8 → 9** adds `director.major.waveBreakers` and a top-level `drops`, stamping a version-8 in-flight assault with zero Breakers so an assault begun without heavies finishes without them. Checks: **615 passed / 0 failed / 1 skipped** of 616 sim tests (`dotnet test` over `Sim/**` + `Tests/Sim/**`, out of the editor), including 9 new focused tests; `Relight.Sim` and the Unity-side assemblies compile clean. Known limitation carried forward from GP-W4: until the owner runs **Relight ▸ Data ▸ Generate tuning assets** there is no `Tuning - Siege.asset` on disk, `DataValidator` reports one missing reference and the sim falls back to `SiegeTuning.Fallback` — the same numbers, so gameplay is correct either way. No Unity Play Mode run has been performed and the owner has not playtested or accepted this row.

- **2026-09-15 — GP-W4 done (engineering; owner acceptance pending):** the major assault is now a PLANNED, SAVED sequence of waves (**U-D-47**). `Sim/Combat/Director/SiegePlan.cs` decides the whole encounter once, when it is scheduled — wave sizes by cumulative rounding of a growth weight so the parts always sum to the exported roster, spitters held back and then distributed by the same rounding so the exported split survives, one wave start per gap, and an approach count that grows wave by wave and is clamped to the approaches the map actually stages. `MajorRaid` carries that plan (`Wave`, `WaveSpawned`, `WaveAnnounced`, `WaveCount`, `WaveSpitters`, `WaveStart`, `WaveApproaches`, `WaveOffset`), so `DirectorPhase.Spawn` became a pure reader of it: a wave can neither duplicate itself nor vanish across a save. Each later wave is announced once, as it opens, naming the quarters it is actually using; the pre-assault warning now names the FIRST wave's quarter plus the wave count and length, so the player is told the shape rather than a coordinate. `Director.Defer` slides the whole plan (`SiegePlan.Shift`) instead of restating a window, and `DirectorRules.Birth` takes an explicit kind so the roster comes from the plan rather than the legacy every-third-body rule. `RaidTuning.WindowS` no longer describes an assault's length anywhere (`OpeningRules.SafeAt` included). Shipped defaults — 4 waves, 110 s apart, a 40 s spread inside each wave and a 30 s tail, spitters from wave 2, one more approach per wave, escalation 15% per assault SURVIVED capped at 2× — give waves of **9 / 12 / 18 / 21** carrying **0 / 5 / 7 / 8** spitters over **400 s**. They are derived, not guessed: the roster is 1800 hp, which is exactly 180 gun-turret rounds (no overkill waste), which is 18 `bullet-batch` = 36 Steel + 18 Copper + 108 s of one Assembler — and three loaded turrets hold only 150, so the siege outlasts loaded hoppers and forces one mid-siege delivery. Spitters reach 7.0 against the turret's 9.0, so they do not outrange turrets; four concentrated on one turret kill it in 5 s, which is why the pressure lever is the growing approach count rather than a longer clock. New Unity-owned tuning (`SiegeTuning`, `SiegeTuningAsset`, `GameDataRegistry.siege`, `DataAssetGenerator`) keeps every parameter editable through the existing data pipeline; until the owner runs **Relight ▸ Data ▸ Generate tuning assets** the registry reports one missing reference and the sim falls back to `SiegeTuning.Fallback`, which is the same numbers. `SaveSchema.Version` 7 → 8: a version-7 in-flight assault is stamped as the single wave it was already sending, because a fresh state has `major: null` and `FillMissing` therefore has no template to default a plan from. Checks: `dotnet build` clean on the sim and Unity-side scratch projects; **606 passed / 0 failed / 1 skipped** of 607 sim tests, including 7 new ones; and the real Home export plans 9/12/18/21 with the approach count honestly clamped to the two staging-valid approaches that region has. No Unity Play Mode run has been performed and the owner has not playtested or accepted this work.
- **2026-09-15 — GP-W3 done (engineering; owner acceptance pending):** raid scheduling, warnings and turret placement feedback repaired under **U-D-45** and **U-D-46**. A minor raid is now announced before it exists: `MinorRaid` carries `StartsAt`, `Owed`, `Spawned` and the `Heading` its bodies will actually arrive on, and it is only staged once `DirectorRules.Staging` has found open ground — a raid that cannot be staged is retried for 30 s and dropped rather than announced against an invalid coordinate. `DirectorRules.Origin`, `Approaches` and `Staging` were rewritten onto one reachability and clearance definition (they previously disagreed, which is why majors announced staging that never arrived), the approach scan box was widened from the core's ±30 tiles to the full 60-step walk, and authored `SiteKind.Camp` sites now join the map's candidate approaches instead of replacing them. Every HUD notice is written through `Director.Say(text, untilT)` and cleared by `Director.Expire`, so a stale "assault announced" cannot outlive its encounter. Tuning is the Unity-owned `SiegeTuning` — **30 s + 0..15 s warning, 30 s staging retry, 45 s notice hold** — held outside the exported `RaidTuning` so the next catalogue export cannot drop it. `SaveSchema.Version` 6 → 7: a version-6 save whose raid was already on the map upgrades to a `Spawned` wave instead of being announced a second time (writing that test found and fixed a genuine migration refusal — `FillMissing` cannot supply `director.minor.heading`, because a fresh director has no minor to copy a default from). Line of fire was made consistent: `Sim/Combat/Sightline.cs` holds the one rule (a wall or barricade stops a shot, both endpoints' own tiles exempt) over a `WallMask` keyed on `SimState.Rev`, and `TurretPhase`, `EnemyQueries.Nearest` and `Ballistics` all use it — previously only the engineer's rifle respected player-built walls. `TurretSight.Coverage` sweeps that same rule over 48 bearings, and the placement preview now draws the ground a turret can actually cover inside a faint nominal ring, with an amber blind/restricted advisory in the build toolbar and matching wording in the opening objective. Checks: **599 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped** (`dotnet test` over `Sim/**` + `Tests/Sim/**`, out of the editor); `Relight.Sim` and `Relight.Game` both compile clean; staging, warning and arrival verified end to end against the shipped Home export in a scratch console harness. No Unity Play Mode run and no owner playtest has been performed for this row.

- **2026-09-16 — GP-UX-4 done (engineering; owner acceptance pending):** a fourth owner UI report — *“The inventory for every producer, can you improve the UI? I have to scroll down to see the inventory slots. The UI can take up more of the screen if needed”* — fixed under **U-D-50**. **Root cause, two independent geometry faults, neither in C#.** `UI/Styles/industrial.uss` imports LAST of the drawer’s five sheets, and it pinned `.inventory-scroll` to `height: 144px` — exactly two rows at the theme’s 69 px slot pitch, whatever the drawer’s height, leaving ~400 px of the drawer unused at 1080p — and gave the drawer 520 px, 800 px with a store open, which is ~365 px of grid a side, five columns, so the forty Backpack slots were eight rows shown two at a time. On top of that the grids sat INSIDE the body `ScrollView` underneath the workshop section and the machine controls, so opening a processor with a recipe put its own slots below the fold: the literal report. **The fix is structural, vertical and horizontal.** `.inventory-pair` is now a direct child of `#inventory-panel` and the growing one; `#backpack-body` is capped at 42% and carries only the machine controls and the collapsed workshop foldout; `.inventory-scroll` is `flex-grow: 1; height: auto; max-height: none` with a one-row floor; the drawer is 900 px alone and 96% capped at 1560 px with a producer open (860 px / 1240 px under `compact-ui`, whose slots drop to 56 px so a narrow window gains columns instead of losing them); and the secondary blocks — side headings, machine status and progress, recipe/filter/priority, the item detail, the equipment strip — were re-laid out as wrapping rows. **No C# was touched:** every element is fetched by name in `InventoryPanelController.Bind`, so the re-parenting is invisible to the controller. **By computation from the shipped values:** at 1920×1080 with a producer open the drawer is 1560 px, each side ~759 px, 10–11 columns — the forty Backpack slots are 4 rows and a producer’s own slots 1 row, neither scrolling, against 2 rows of 8 before; at 1280×720 the band is only 520 px tall so the Backpack still scrolls, by about one row (3 of 4 rows at 10 columns, against 2 of 8 at 5 columns). **Checks:** 617/618 sim tests still pass (1 long-run skip; no sim code changed); the scratch csproj compiling `Sim/**`, `Data/**`, `World/**`, `Presentation/**`, `UI/**` and `Tests/Play/**` against the installed editor’s managed assemblies builds clean with the same 3 pre-existing CS0252 warnings, so the 4 new Play-mode tests in `Tests/Play/Ui/InventoryLayoutTests.cs` are known to compile; both UXML files parse as well-formed XML and every element name the tests look up is present. Two stale class doc comments in `Tests/Play/Ui/{UiFixture,InventoryDragPlayTests}.cs` that still claimed the Play tests had never been compiled were corrected. **Unity Play Mode is not available on this machine: the layout tests have NOT been run, no screenshot or live measurement was taken at 1080p, 720p or 125%, and the owner has not playtested or accepted this work.** Changes remain uncommitted.
- **2026-09-16 — GP-UX-5 done (engineering; owner acceptance pending):** the owner rejected what GP-UX-4 shipped — *“That’s not what I wanted. I want the players inventory to be on the left and the producer to be on the right. The Producers UI needs to be redone a little to have the slots at the top. Do you know what I mean?”* — and the drawer was rebuilt under **U-D-51**, which amends U-D-50(a). **Checked before assuming, and the check changed the fix:** the Backpack was ALREADY the left column and the producer the right (`InventoryPanel.uxml` orders `#side-pack` before `#side-store`; no `row-reverse` anywhere), so the first sentence described what the owner expected to see rather than asking for a flip. The actual fault was the third thing on the screen: GP-UX-4 had lifted the slot grids out of the scrolling body but LEFT that body there, under both columns, still carrying every producer's controls and the Home workshop as a collapsed foldout — so a producer's UI was split between the right column and a strip beneath it, and the workshop was not a producer at all. The owner was offered the two candidate shapes as options and chose “Producer column owns its controls” and “Right column, like any producer”. **What changed.** `#backpack-body`, `#workshop-foldout` and the `workshop-view` mode are gone; `#inventory-panel` is a heading row, `.inventory-pair` and the pinned footer. The left column is purely the player (Backpack heading, grid, equipment strip, quickbar) and always wears its own name. The right column IS the open producer: `#store-inventory` — heading, capacity, slot grid, hint — and then `#store-body` with the status, the progress bar, repair, recipe / item filter / output priority and the description, plus the workshop section. The Home workshop is now a producer like any other: its 20 tray slots first, the three recipe cards under them (`WorkshopPanel.uxml` reordered, `.workshop-heading` un-hidden and the section's own frame dropped, since the column is its frame), reached from the drawer heading's **Home workshop** button, which hides while the workshop is what the column is holding. Where the column must be divided the slots win — `store-machine` caps the controls at 46% (52% under `compact-ui`) — except at Home, where the depot's storage AND the workshop are open at once and the cap comes off so the two halves divide the column instead of one being crushed. `storage-open` now means “the right column has a producer in it”, so a machine with no storage at all (a belt: controls only) still gets the wide drawer. **Drawer widths, slot pitch and the `compact-ui` rules are unchanged from GP-UX-4.** **C# changes are small and structural:** `SetWorkshopView` is retired, `Bind` picks up `#store-inventory`, `Paint` decides the column's contents, and `OpenStore` resets `#store-body`'s scroll offset so a producer just opened starts at its slots. **By computation from the shipped values, not observed:** at 1920×1080 with a producer open the drawer is 1560 px, each side about 758 px, about 744 px of grid after the 14 px scroller → 10 columns at the 69 px pitch; the forty Backpack slots are 4 rows and a machine's slots 1 row; the pair is about 720 px tall, so a machine's controls get about 331 px against the roughly 250 px they need, and the workshop case gets the whole 720 px. **Checks:** 617/618 sim tests still pass (1 long-run skip; no sim code changed); the scratch csproj compiling `Sim/**`, `Data/**`, `World/**`, `Presentation/**`, `UI/**` and `Tests/Play/**` against the installed editor's managed assemblies builds clean with the same 3 pre-existing CS0252 warnings, so the Play-mode tests — `TheProducerColumnOwnsItsControls` (replacing GP-UX-4's test of the old shape) and the new `TheWorkshopTrayIsAboveItsRecipeCards`, alongside the three kept from GP-UX-4 — are known to compile; both UXML files parse as well-formed XML, every element name the controllers and tests look up is present, and `backpack-body` / `workshop-foldout` / `workshop-view` survive nowhere but in explanatory comments. **Unity Play Mode is not available on this machine: the layout tests have NOT been run, no screenshot or live measurement was taken at 1080p, 720p or 125%, and the owner has not playtested or accepted this work.** Changes remain uncommitted.

- **2026-09-16 — GP-UX-6 done (engineering; owner acceptance pending):** a third report in the drawer series, and the first that accepts the shape it corrects — *“Can you make it so the inventory on the producer is fixed at the top and only the recipes are scrollable? Make the recipes fit 2 horizontally per row instead of 1.”* — implemented as **U-D-52**, which amends U-D-51(c). **What was actually wrong.** U-D-51 put the producer’s slots at the top and its controls in a `#store-body` scroller beneath them, but the slots themselves still had `flex-grow: 1`, so the column divided by a 46% cap on the controls rather than by the slots taking what they need; and because `#store-body` was ONE scroller, at Home the workshop’s 20-slot output tray scrolled together with its recipe cards — the tray is slots, and slots were supposed to have stopped scrolling. **What changed.** `#store-inventory` is `flex-grow: 0` and its grid (`#store-scroll`) sits between a one-row 72 px floor and a 300 px ceiling, so a forty-slot chest still cannot push the controls off the bottom; `#store-body` takes everything left and is a plain `VisualElement` now, with each producer bringing the scroller its own contents need — a new `#machine-scroll` around a machine’s controls, and `#workshop-cards` itself a `ScrollView` so the workshop’s tray stays fixed with the other slots. The `store-machine` class and both caps (46%, 52% compact) are gone. Cards are two to a row: the row and wrap moved onto the scroller’s content container and a `.recipe-host` is `flex-basis: 50%` / `flex-grow: 0` with its 8 px gutter as PADDING, because Yoga measures `flex-basis` border-box and a margin would have wrapped the second card away; `min-width: 180px` is the one-per-row fallback. The new USS is written as **id** rules in `InventoryPanel.uss` so it wins over `industrial.uss` by specificity instead of by import order. **By computation from the shipped values, not observed:** at 1920×1080 the right column is about 758 px wide, the cards’ scroller about 730 px across, a host about 365 px and a card about 357 px against a 232 px design width; vertically the column is about 720 px, a one-row producer grid takes about 130 px and `#store-body` about 590 px, so a machine’s roughly 250 px of controls never scroll and the workshop’s cards keep roughly 360 px under the tray. **Checks:** 617/618 sim tests unchanged (1 long-run skip; no sim code touched); the scratch csproj compiling `Sim/**`, `Data/**`, `World/**`, `Presentation/**`, `UI/**` and `Tests/Play/**` against the installed editor’s managed assemblies rebuilds clean with the same 3 pre-existing CS0252 warnings, so the two new Play-mode tests — `AProducersSlotsAreFixedAtTheTopOfItsColumn` and `TheRecipeCardsSitTwoToARow` — are known to compile; both UXML files parse as well-formed XML and all three stylesheets balance. **Unity Play Mode is not available on this machine: no layout test has been RUN, no screenshot or live measurement was taken at 1080p, 720p or 125%, and the owner has not playtested or accepted this work.** Changes remain uncommitted.
- **2026-09-16 — GP-UX-6 rule (d): equal-height recipe cards (engineering; owner acceptance pending):** a fourth report, arriving after the rule (c) work was reported — *“The recipe cards need to have the same height each. At the moment they’re all different”* — added to **U-D-52** as rule (d) rather than opened as a decision of its own, because it corrects what rule (c) did: two to a row is what made ragged heights visible, a short card now sitting BESIDE a tall one instead of above it. **What was actually wrong.** `industrial.uss` imports last and held `.recipe-card { height: auto }` — a live-inspection fix from when a card was a full-width row of its own and stretching one meant stretching it down the whole column — which defeated `WorkshopPanel.uss`’s `height: 100%` and left every card sized to its own text. **What changed.** The card now fills its host with `flex-grow: 1` in both sheets (not a percentage height: the host has no height of its own, it takes the row’s, so a percentage would resolve against a parent still being measured), and `.recipe-host` carries `min-height: 152px` in both. The two mechanisms do different jobs: stretch within a wrapped row squares the pair off against each other, and only the floor squares one row off against the next, because `align-content` is `flex-start` and wrapped rows are measured independently. The card’s bottom margin is gone, so the host’s padding is the only gutter. **By arithmetic on the shipped values, not observed:** 16 px padding + 2 px border + ~21 px title + 58 px ingredient row + 9 px bar + ~18 px for a one-line note ≈ 124 px of card, plus ~15 px for a second wrapped note line, plus the host’s 8 px bottom gutter ≈ 147 px, rounded up to 152; `.compact-ui` trades 4 px of padding for a taller title, so it needs no figure of its own. A recipe that outgrows the floor takes its own row taller and is a failure, not a silent return to ragged: the new `TheRecipeCardsAreAllTheSameHeight` asserts that every card fills its host and that every host matches the first, and reports the height the floor would have to clear. **Checks:** 617/618 sim tests unchanged (1 long-run skip; no sim code touched); the scratch csproj rebuilds clean with the same 3 pre-existing CS0252 warnings, so the new test is known to compile; all three stylesheets still balance their braces and comments. **Unity Play Mode is not available on this machine: the test has NOT been run, no screenshot or live measurement was taken at any resolution, the 152 px is arithmetic rather than a measured tallest card, and the owner has not playtested or accepted this work.** Changes remain uncommitted.

- **2026-09-16 — GP-OPEN-1 done (engineering; owner acceptance pending):** the owner asked what the opening objective ladder got wrong (*“OK which ones need fixing?”*), was given four ranked fixes, and authorised them verbatim — *“Give the foundry no default. Do the rest”*. **U-D-53.** (a) The Foundry, Assembler and Assembler Mk2 now ship with an empty `DefaultRecipe`, overridden in `OpeningBalance.Apply` because both data paths run through it, so the generated catalogue and the editor `.asset` files are covered without either being touched; ladder rows 10 and 28 were completing themselves the moment the machine was placed, since their guards test the recipe and the data already supplied one. (b) `opening-ammo` split into a build row and a “Set up the Assembler / Select the Bullets recipe” row, because with no default “built but unset” is a state the player can sit in and the old row would have kept saying “Build 1 Assembler” to someone who had built one. (c) `OpeningPhase.NoteSupply` now also latches “Automatic resupply working” off a turret with no room whose supplier still resolves: both write sites of `Stats.TurretFed` require hopper room, so a player keeping the turret full by hand had a working belt and a permanently stalled chain; the walk stays gated on `ProducedAt >= 0` and on `OpeningRules.TurretSupplier`, so a hand load cannot fake it, and the cheap test runs first. (d) the unreachable `Words[0] = “no”` deleted. **621 passed / 0 failed / 1 skipped of 622** (four new tests; ten existing production, power and persistence tests given an explicit recipe through a new `ProductionFixture.Recipe` helper, since they test the production loop rather than the catalogue), engine-side rebuild clean apart from the 3 pre-existing CS0252 warnings. **Play Mode is not available on this machine, so nothing here has been seen running and no screenshot was taken.** Two things the owner should know: an existing v9 save whose Foundry or Assembler was running on the data default loads that machine **idle** until a recipe is chosen — nothing recorded what it was making, and `SaveUpgrade` cannot repair a v9→v9 load without a schema bump that was not authorised; and the **Refinery, Mixer and Alien workbench keep their defaults**, which the opening never names but which leaves the port inconsistent about whether a placed machine starts with a recipe.
- **2026-09-16 — GP-PLAYTEST-1 done (engineering; owner acceptance pending):** the owner asked for a simulated first thirty minutes (*“do a playtest of the first 30 minutes. simulate a new player experience and tell me any errors, worrying spots, anything missing and improvements that can be made”*), read the ranked report, and authorised the fixes — *“OK lets fix these”*. **U-D-54. Seven of the eight shipped; two of the report’s recommendations were corrected on their premises, one reshaped and one withdrawn.** The run is a headless objective playthrough driven through sim commands; it establishes that these defects exist and nothing about balance or normal progression. **What the run actually showed, with the evidence:** it ended `Home core … hp 0/300 DISABLED` beside `opening encounter: Repelled shots 0` — a turret that never fired, and a “win” printed over a dead core; `chest #4 … inv ironore 200` (exactly `ChestCap`) beside `foundry #10 … state OutputFull`, the shared-chest deadlock, with the chain still calling both belts connected; the literal HUD line “hold fire without power. no power. Poles connect…”; “Fuel your Generator” recurring at 02:08, 13:45 and 22:49 with the coal receding 15 → 33 → 28 tiles; and “Mine Coal salvage 33 tiles south-east”, which Coal is not. **One recommendation in the published report was wrong and is corrected here.** It asked for the attack direction to be named when the turret is built. `DirectorQueries.OpeningOrigin(ctx, st, turretId)` requires a placed turret and scores `DirectorRules.Origin` + `Approaches` by `Clearance`, picking the origin whose breach path passes NEAREST the turret — the direction is derived FROM where the player put the turret, so it cannot be named earlier, and the log shows it already named once the group is announced (“about 5 basic enemies from the south marker”). The real gap is the case the director cannot fix: the nearest of a poor set can still be far out of range, and the only way to discover that was to watch the group walk past a silent turret. The new `Uncovered` helper reuses the same `Clearance` and says so on the Scheduled and Active rows. **A second recommendation was withdrawn on its premise.** The report said Shift did nothing on a walk-here. It does not — but `WorldInput` submits no `MoveCommand` anywhere, because C-02/R-CP-3 deleted the empty-hand walk-here on purpose (its doc comment: “if one reappears, click-to-move is back”). The finding came out of the harness, which drives `MoveCommand` directly; no player can reach that path. The one-line widening was written and then backed out rather than shipped, because it would have changed sim behaviour on a deliberately retired route for tests and headless runs alone. **Both corrections are the same lesson the owner has already taught once — check the premise before fixing it — and both were caught by reading the code the report was describing.** **A judgment call stated plainly:** the fuel treadmill was answered with teaching text and honest arithmetic — one Coal ≈ 13 s at the full 300 kW, the slot holds 50, and an Excavator on a Coal patch belted into the Generator ends hand-feeding for good — rather than by raising what one hand load buys. **No balance change, no save-schema change, and no new blocking row**: the chest-deadlock row fires only once the chest is half full of ore or the Foundry already reports `OutputFull`, so a working base is never nagged. **Checks:** **629 passed / 0 failed / 1 skipped of 630**, up from 621/622 by the eight tests in the new `Tests/Sim/Regression/PlaytestFixTests.cs`; the U9 core-repair test was **not** loosened — it guards a real contract, so the production text was written back to contain its phrase, and only three tile-wording assertions in `ObjectiveSourceTests.cs` were updated; the engine-side scratch csproj rebuilds (`-t:Rebuild`, for an honest count) with the same 3 pre-existing CS0252 warnings. **Unity Play Mode is not available on this machine: none of this has been seen running, no screenshot was taken, and the owner has not playtested or accepted it.** Changes remain uncommitted.

- **2026-09-17 — GP-UX-7 done (engineering; owner acceptance pending):** four unrelated bullets from the owner, and the pass is a good argument for reading before fixing. The plate-production objective was not failing to notice a working base — it was refusing one, because `ReachesKind` hopped a single belt and the owner’s buffer chest answered “chest” where the guard wanted “foundry”; the walk now passes through `BufferKind`, the one kind `FlowRules.Wants` accepts unconditionally and `FlowPhase.SourceCount` drains. Making it transitive immediately endangered U-D-54’s shared-chest deadlock row — every chest downstream of an Excavator is now “reached” from it — so that row was narrowed to a direct shared sink (`DirectEnds`/`SharedSink`) and now weighs the ore in that chest rather than the fullest chest on the map. “No power” was being drawn twice: `HudViewModel` posted an infinite-TTL notice on every 150 ms refresh and `HudNotices.Post` counts a repeat whenever the row it replaces is still live, so the toast ticked its own counter ~6.7×/s next to a strip that already said it; the post is gone and the key is cleared. The second half is the more interesting half — the strip the toast was duplicating had been grouped with `.notice-row` in `industrial.uss`, which repainted its ground to #202920 and left Hud.uss’s `color: var(--ui-bg)` (#151d1e) sitting on it: ≈1.2:1, so the owner had never actually seen it, which is exactly why the toast read as the only message. It now owns a deep-red ground with light bold text. Tab showing the Home workshop was a sentinel collision: `UiShell.ToggleBackpack` and `WorldInput.Interact` both pass machine `-1`, so the id could not say which route the drawer arrived by — the caller says it now (`OpenWorkshop()` beside `OpenStore(id)`), and `GameplayDock`’s Backpack tooltip stopped promising a workshop that Tab no longer opens. The build cards took the tested U-D-52 rule (d) host/card pattern, with the two numbers measured out of `Inter-Regular.ttf` because Play Mode cannot be run here: the worst cost string is 458px at 16px, which wrapped to a fourth line in the 156px column beside the icon and fits three in the ≥174px column the restacked card gives it. One trap came with the host and was caught before finishing — a host holds its 144px floor whether or not its card is on the open tab, so `PaintCard` had to hide both, and the new Play test asserts every shown host still contains a visible card. **Nothing in this row has been seen running.**
- **2026-09-17 — GP-UX-8 done (engineering; owner acceptance pending):** two reports, and both were fixed by removing code rather than adding it. Pressing E with the rifle out *“does something weird. I can't inspect things”*: `Interact` asked what the HAND held before it asked what the player was pointing at, and the weapon branch returned early — so E opened the machine under the cursor only when the pointer was already on it, never opened the Home workshop the HUD advertises, printed no notice at all, and silently toggled the rifle in and out of equipment slot 0. The unequip half is what made it *weird*: the rifle stayed in hand and on the dock while `WeaponPhase` began refusing every shot with “Equip a Rifle”. The reference binds E to interaction and nothing else, so the equip role had no decision behind it and is gone; the settings label now says **Interact**, while the input action keeps its asset name `Equip` so no player loses a saved rebinding. Second: *“when the turret fires… it looks like the player shoots”* — the turret’s own muzzle was recorded correctly by the sim, but `TracerPresenter` re-anchored the NEWEST tracer to the engineer and the shot list is shared with every turret, so a turret’s shot was drawn leaving the player. Every tracer now starts where the sim put it, which also stops the player’s own tracer sliding along with them for its half second. No `Sim/**` file changed. Engine-side rebuild: 0 errors, the 3 pre-existing CS0252 warnings. Play Mode is unavailable here, so the new Play test compiles and has not been run and nothing has been seen running. See DECISIONS.md U-D-56.
- **2026-09-17 — GP-UX-9 done (engineering; owner acceptance pending):** the sixth drawer report, and the clearest case yet for checking the premise before fixing it. Two of the three asks already existed — Sort in three places, splitting as a button, a menu entry and a sim command — so the work was to put them where a player looks and to give splitting a destination the player chooses. The third ask, the one that read as a gameplay bug, was not in the sim at all: a probe run against the real handlers accepted a move from slot 0 to slot 20 in a Backpack with no recorded layout and put the stack in slot 20, which is the state a new game is in. The drawer was losing the drop. `#pack-grid` is a wrapping grid inside a ScrollView, so `panel.Pick` on a release a few pixels off a slot returns the grid, the gutter or the viewport — not a slot — and `Drop` did `Say(""); return;`. A null `Engineer.Pack` is displayed auto-compacted, so every stack already sits at the front and a lost drop is indistinguishable from an unwanted sort. Fixed by resolving the picked element FIRST and falling back to the nearest slot in the grid the pointer is inside (grid as bound, clipped to the scroll viewport, so no cross-column snapping and no snapping to a slot that is scrolled out of sight), and by saying `DropOffSlot` when the release really was off the grids. Sort gained a copy on the Backpack header, always enabled; the footer copy and `menu-sort` stay. Shift-drag splits into the hovered empty slot for the Quantity field's amount or half, previewing the sim's own refusal on the ghost before release; the Split BUTTON keeps first-empty, because a button has no destination to read. Two sim regressions now cover the never-arranged state and a chosen split slot — 635 passed / 0 failed / 1 skipped of 636 — and three Play tests cover a gutter release, a Shift-drag split and a release off the grids; those compile but cannot be run here.

- 2026-09-22 — **A lost large raid now ends (E-18, REL-44; `ENEMY-THREAT-AUDIT.md` ENM-01 to ENM-03). Sim code and tests; no UI; no owner play.** *What changed:* (1) a Home core at 0 hit points is no longer a target: `EnemyCoreHook.Rect` refuses it, and only a region whose core was never placed still falls back to the authored site. (2) When the core falls, a committed large raid ends on that tick: its unspawned waves are cancelled, it is written to the history, the recovery spell starts, and the next large raid waits a full interval (`IntervalMinS` plus the seeded range) from that moment. A small raid that beats the core is called off the same way. (3) The survivors walk off; a survivor of an earlier raid never joins the next one (`EnemyPhase.Decide` checks the group), and any still on the map 120 s after the raid ended is removed without a kill (U-P-21). (4) A large raid still alive 300 s past its planned end is broken off (U-P-20). (5) Core repair is blocked only by a large-raid or small-raid body within 12 tiles of the core (U-P-19), not by any raider on the map. **Interim, owned by F1-30 (not decided here):** the history gains an `Outcome` of Cleared, Lost or BrokeOff. BrokeOff is neutral. Sim code, tuning, save schema, tests; sim tests only, no owner play.** *Now:* new `DirectorPacing` holds the rules the director asks before it speaks. After a large raid's recovery comes a quiet spell of 480 s with no warning and no small raid (U-P-17). Each cycle has one small raid after the quiet spell; the next large-raid warning waits, silently, for it to arrive, end and clear the 120 s gap after it, and stops waiting 600 s after the quiet spell so a small raid that cannot happen never stops the clock (U-P-22). The large raid's own warning is always the full one. A small raid does not start while the engineer is down or a camp resident is chasing them, for at most 180 s (U-P-23). One whose target is more than 150 tiles from the engineer gets 30 s more warning (U-P-24). The campaign's first small raid cannot take the Home core below half its hit points; at the floor it breaks off with “The core held. The raid … is breaking off.” (`RaidNoticeKind.CoreHeld`, U-D-68 (b), U-P-25); a committed large raid's damage is never softened by it. The stronghold rule (U-D-69 (e)) is built behind `DirectorPacing.StrongholdFight`, which nothing answers until the Freight work adds strongholds, so it is always false today. The Admin pull-in waives the holds that are only a matter of time, as it already waived the interval. New `SiegeTuning` values (all the assistant's placeholders under U-D-28) are in `Tuning - Siege.asset` and `THREAT_TUNING.md`; data hash 0be94136 → 475be32e. `DirectorState` gains the quiet-spell end, the cycle's small-raid count, the last small raid's end and when a due small raid was first held back, and a small raid its floor: save schema 11 → 12 (`SaveUpgrade.ElevenToTwelve`: a v11 game owes no quiet spell or hold, and a raid already under way gets no floor). *Cadence:* with raids of ordinary length a large raid now comes about every 27–31 minutes after the second, not U-D-38's nominal 18–22; U-D-38 carries a dated note, and the change is a tuning call under U-D-28. *Tests:* `DirectorPacingTests` (new, 16, on the flat director fixture; they pin the rules and are not a playtest of the pacing). Adapted to the intended change: `RaidFailurePathTests` (runs the clock through the capped wait for the cycle's small raid, which that director-only bench never ends), `RaidAccountTests` (its small raid now outlasts the hold before the large raid is pulled in), and the three real-city EditMode runs (`RealCityRaidTests` twice and `RealCityIntegrationTests` IC-1), which pull the next large raid in through the new `RealCityFixture.PullInLargeRaid`; it waives the new holds exactly as the Admin pull-in does and waits `WarningWait` for the commit. - 2026-09-23 — **Event autosaves at a raid's warning and its end (REL-65, AUD-PER-05; TECHNICAL_ARCHITECTURE.md §9.4.2). Sim and Presentation code, tests; sim tests only, no owner play.** *Was:* `AutosaveScheduler.Trigger` existed and nothing called it, so saves were timed only. *Now:* `AutosaveScheduler.Observe` reads the frame's sim events, and `AutosaveController` calls it at every tick boundary before the timed clock. It writes one ring save when a raid's warning opens (`RaidNoticeEvent` `Announced` or `MinorRaid`, once per raid, so a large raid's later wave lines and a repeated warning save nothing) and one when any raid ends (`RaidEndedEvent`). A group staged at once with no warning (the opening's encounter, the Admin raid) saves only at its end. Several moments in one frame are one save, and each save resets the five-minute timer. *Guard:* no event save while the engineer is down; the save is owed and written on the first frame they are up. The timed ring and `auto-quit.json` are not guarded and behave as before. A new session, including a load, forgets the owed save; a raid already on the map in a loaded game counts as warned, so its next wave line is not a new start. "Site restored or plant commissioned" has no sim event yet and is not wired; the code says so. *Judgement calls, to keep or reverse:* "raid start" is read as the warning opening, not the first body arriving, matching the issue's first wording ("the raid-warned edge") and §9.4.2's "before a wave"; and the guard delays an event save until the engineer is up rather than dropping it. §9.4.2 and `TASK-REMAINING.md` (AUD-PER-05, F1-20) carry dated notes; the settings tooltip and doc comments no longer say "nothing calls it yet". *Tests:* `EventAutosaveTests` (new, 9): on the real director on the flat fixture, one save at the warning, none for the wave lines, one at the end; a small raid's warning and end; repeated warnings and other notices save nothing; two moments in a frame are one save; an event save resets the timer; no save while down, then one when up; the setting off saves and owes nothing; a reset forgets; a game loaded mid-raid does not save its next wave line. The existing `AutosaveTests` pass unchanged. What was wrong was that one state had two words, and that “2 wrecks” beside “1 turret dry” in one sentence reads as two wrecked TURRETS, so a wrecked wall was reported as a gun. *Built:* `StateText(Disabled)` now says “wrecked (0 hp)”, the word the world badge, the repair card and the raid account already use; the defence sentence says “N structures wrecked”, the raid account’s own word for the same tally. *Scope deviation, stated:* the issue’s scope line says “the HUD view-model only”, but the HUD prints `StateText` verbatim by design — a second vocabulary is the defect itself — so the one word had to change at the producer; had it been patched in the HUD, the machine panel and the world hover would still say “disabled”. `MachineOperatingState.Disabled` keeps its name and its saved value. *U-D-61 unchanged:* the defence row still counts every machine at 0 hit points at the place, walls and conveyors included; it now says so. *Tests:* one new — `AWreckedTurretAndAWreckedWallReadTheSameWayInBothRows` — covering the issue’s own case (one wrecked turret, one wrecked wall, one dry turret): no row says “disabled”, a wreck ranks first, the defence row is one row for the place, its `Wrecks` equals the machines the problem rows speak for, and its sentence never reads “turrets wrecked”. an editor script read the real first substation site out of the running game and handed the HUD one event for it with `Returning` false and then true — presentation staging only, with the sim rule that decides which is which covered by the tests. *Not done:* nobody has played this build; - 2026-09-23 — **A turret boxed in by the buildings says so on its own card (REL-15, INT-11).
