- 2026-09-23 — **Three small holes in the blind badge and the defence row (REL-14, INT-10). Sim and Presentation code with four new tests, one decision (U-D-70); not played.** *Premise checked first, and all three parts held.* (a) `TurretQueries.Blind` returned early on `u.Target != 0`, with “it has no target” written into its doc comment as deliberate — so a gun firing at the skitter in front of it while a Spitter shelled it from the unlit street was the one case the eye-with-a-slash badge refused to show, although the guide line it raises says exactly what to do about it (“Light the ground it guards”). The early-out is gone; the sweep can never name the gun’s own target, because `TurretRules.Reach` only reaches an unlit body INSIDE dark sight and every body the sweep considers is unlit and beyond it. (b) `DefenceAlertSource` raised a place only when something there ran dry, or ran low during a raid — documented as intentional (“Wrecks alone never raise one”) — so the worst case of all was the quiet one: every gun at a site knocked down in one raid, none having run out first, nothing dry, no row. A wrecked TURRET now raises its place (`DefenceAlertRow.TurretWrecks`); a wrecked wall or belt alone still raises nothing, and the sentence still counts every structure at 0 hit points (REL-53). Recorded as **U-D-70**, which narrows one clause of U-D-61 — NOT an owner decision, and the turret-only restriction on raising is this port’s reading of U-D-61’s intent. (c) `MachineActivityVisual` ran the full query per turret per frame while `TurretPhase` was already storing the identical answer once per tick in `TurretUnit.Blind` for the blind event; the badge now reads it through `TurretQueries.BlindNow`, so it also can never disagree with the guide line. Measured in the offline build, 12 turrets under fire with 60 bodies: 24 µs per frame before, below the 1 µs measurement resolution after — the shape of the saving, not a frame-rate claim, and the benchmark was written in the scratchpad and deleted so it cannot pollute the counts. The query was already cheap for a turret NOT hit in the last three seconds, so this is a raid-time cost, paid when the frame budget is tightest. *Checks:* offline sim 929 passed, 1 skipped (was 925+1); EditMode 1025/1025 (was 1021); PlayMode 97 total, 92 passed, 0 failed, 5 skipped (unchanged). Four new tests: `ATurretFiringAtOneAlienIsStillBlindToTheOneShellingItFromTheDark` and `TheLatchedAnswerFollowsTheQueryTickByTick` in `TurretDarkSightTests`, `APlaceWhoseTurretsAreAllWreckedIsReported` and `ANonTurretWreckIsCountedOnceTheTurretHasRaisedThePlace` in `DefenceAlertTests`; the old `WrecksAloneNeverRaiseARow` is kept, renamed `AWreckedWallOrBeltAloneNeverRaisesARow`, because that half of the rule still holds. *Not done:* no screenshots — the issue’s acceptance criterion is tests, and both signals would need a staged raid to photograph. Nobody has played this build. [Evidence](evidence/rel-14/README.md). Next free ids: U-D-71, U-P-29, E-25, F1-39.
- 2026-09-23 — **What the HUD forgets on load, and the tick of ghost bullets after one (REL-62, PER-02 + ENM-07). Sim code with three new tests; not played.** *Premise checked first.* PER-02 held only in part: REL-14 had already made a dry turret, a low turret during a raid and a wrecked TURRET raise their place again on the first refresh after a load, so what was actually left was the one case none of those cover — a place whose turrets are all loaded and standing and whose only damage left is wrecked walls or belts. That row vanished on a load. ENM-07 held exactly as written: the ballistics seam is transient, only `EnemyPhase` re-pointed it, the weapon phase runs first, and `EnemyTests` set the seam by hand, which is why nothing caught it. (a) `EnemyTargets.Point(SimState)` is now the ONE place that knows how to point the seam; the phase, the initialiser and `Simulation.Wrap` all call it, so a loaded game shoots on its first tick instead of firing one tick of ghost bullets (50 ms — a correctness fix, not a felt one), and no test has to reach in to make shooting work. (b) `Simulation.Wrap` raises the transient `SimState.Resumed`; it is NOT in `Visit`, so it is in neither the save nor the hash, and no phase reads it. On its first refresh against a resumed state `DefenceAlertSource` seeds `_raised` from the places carrying a wreck, so the standing row the player quit with is there again. The load has to be told apart from a fresh start because seeding on *any* first refresh would make a wrecked wall raise a row in a game being played, which is the half of U-D-61 that REL-14 deliberately kept. **Save schema unchanged; no new decision.** The stated price: a place whose wall was wrecked while its row had never been raised now speaks after a load — the world records the wreck, not whether the player was told. *Checks:* offline sim 932 passed, 1 skipped (was 929+1); EditMode 1028/1028 (was 1025); PlayMode 97 total, 89 passed, 3 failed, 5 skipped (was 92 passed, 0 failed, 5 skipped). The three were taken one at a time: `OpeningUi` U6 passes on its own and is order-dependent as before; `EscapeCancelsRepair` was **not** re-run on its own, that run was abandoned; and the `DroppedCargo` E-collect fails on its own as well, so it was run again with every REL-62 change stashed out of the tree, on unmodified `f5f73e79`, where **it fails with the same message**. Run alone it fails either way, so that control cannot tell the two full-suite runs apart, and no full PlayMode suite was run on unmodified code — the drop-collect failure is open and undiagnosed, recorded as its own defect (REL-125), neither fixed nor claimed to be caused here. Three new tests: `ALoadedGameHitsBodiesOnItsFirstTick` (a bolt in the air over a save, one tick in the real Weapons→Turrets→Enemies→Director order, and the test never points the seam itself), `AStandingWreckRowIsBackOnTheFirstRefreshAfterALoad` and `AWreckedWallInAGameThatWasNeverLoadedStillSaysNothing`. Both of the first two were run against the reverted code and both failed, so they bite. *Not done:* the guide-line “already taught” flags and the open raid tally are still per session. They cannot be re-derived from the world — whether a player has been told something is not written in it — so fixing them needs the save schema this issue holds at its current version by design; that decision is not taken here. (Corrected 2026-09-23 while starting REL-66: this entry and the REL-62 commit message both quoted the issue's "9". `SaveSchema.Version` is **12**; the issue's number was stale. No persistence file is in REL-62's diff, so "unchanged" was and is true.) Nobody has played this build. [Evidence](evidence/rel-62/README.md). Next free ids: U-D-71, U-P-29, E-25, F1-39.
- 2026-09-23 — **What a load has to tell the player (REL-66, PER-06). One on-screen route replacing two broken ones, with four new tests; not played.** *Premise checked first, and it was wrong in detail.* The issue says the sentences “are written with `Debug.Log` only” and asks for that reading to be confirmed. It is not accurate: two on-screen routes already existed and both were broken. `FrontEndBootstrap.Collect` put them into `StartupNotice` through an `else if` chain — at most **one** of the three sentences, and only if and when the player next opened the pause menu, i.e. a *startup* notice shown at an unrelated moment about a *load*. `PauseMenuController.DoLoad` wrote the recovery sentence into `_loadNotice` and then switched the panel and closed the overlay in the same call, so nothing was ever on screen long enough to read, and that route never carried the other two at all. The balance-data sentence — “this save was made with different balance data”, the one that says recipe costs may have moved under the player — had **no** on-screen route of any kind. Both routes are retired here and replaced by one. `HudViewModel.SayLoad(LoadResult, now)` says each non-empty sentence as its own HUD row: recovery (`load:recovered`) and balance data (`load:warning`) as Warning, upgrade/region move/heal-on-load (`load:upgraded`) as Info. Three rows, not a chain, because they are independent facts and an `else if` drops the ones it does not reach — exactly the old bug. `LoadSentence` capitalises the store's words and ends them with a stop and reworks nothing else: the save layer owns what is true about the file. They post through `Teach`, the standing guide-row path (REL-118), so a burst of raid rows can only *delay* a load sentence, never expire it unread — **“once” means once seen, not once timed out**. The `LoadResult` INSTANCE marks it said, so `HudController.Paint` hands the same load over on every ordinary paint with no timing of its own, and a HUD that only wakes *after* the load still says it — which neither old route could. A refusal is deliberately not said on the HUD: it changed nothing and the Load screen is already showing the reason on the screen the player is looking at. `AutosaveController` is **unchanged**, so all three sentences are still written to the log, as accept criterion 1 requires. The issue's “build it once here and share it” for ECO-05 is met: the route takes a `LoadResult` and nothing else. *Checks:* offline sim 936 passed, 1 skipped (was 932+1); EditMode 1032/1032 (was 1028); PlayMode 97 total, 89 passed, 3 failed, 5 skipped — **identical to the REL-62 baseline**, the same three tests with the same three messages, so nothing here made them worse or better (the drop-collect one is open as REL-125). PlayMode was run although the issue asks only for EditMode, because this adds work to `HudController.Paint`. Four new tests in `Tests/Sim/UI/LoadNoticeTests.cs`; three of them load **real files off a real disk** in a throwaway folder under the system temp path (the `RealDiskRecoveryTests` pattern), so the fixtures are outside the repository altogether, as accept criterion 2 asks: a forged version-11 document, a primary made unreadable with its `.bak` intact, and a file re-stamped with a foreign `dataVersion`. The fourth pins that two sentences at once are **both** said, that five further hand-overs of the same result add nothing, that the rows retire once seen rather than on a clock, and that a refusal says nothing. *Stated limit:* accept criterion 3 (“the notice does not pause the game or move the camera”) is argued, not tested — `HudViewModel` holds no engine reference and can reach neither, but **no test here proves it**. No new decision, no save-schema change. Nobody has played this build. [Evidence](evidence/rel-66/README.md). Next free ids: U-D-71, U-P-29, E-25, F1-39.
- 2026-09-23 — **A light reaches a quarter further, and the torch two tiles (REL-126, PLAY-01). Sim and Presentation code with three new tests, one placeholder row (U-P-29); not played.** The first of the owner's ten notes from their 2026-09-22 play: “I think the lighting radius should be a little bigger.” Asked which light, they answered **both**. *The owner named no figure* — the quarter and the torch's 14 are the implementer's under U-D-28, recorded as **U-P-29**. Lamp 4→5 tiles, Arc lamp 6→7.5, Floodlight cone 12→15 (longer, **not** wider — the half-angle is untouched), authored kerb streetlight 7→8.75, `MouseFlashlightPresenter.ReachTiles` 12→14. The torch is deliberately not the same quarter: at 15 it would out-reach an Arc lamp, and the point of building a lamp is that it beats carrying your own light. *Where it lives, and why not the obvious place:* the tuning asset is what the GAME reads and `CatalogueData.g.cs` — generated from `packages/`, which this project may not edit — is what every offline sim test reads, so editing the asset alone would have left the tests seeing 4 and the game seeing 5. The change goes in `Sim/Data/DarkWorld.cs`, the layer applied last in **both** chains, which already owns the port's “there is no sun” (U-D-58) and each turret's dark sight (U-D-59); the asset keeps the reference's radii exactly as it keeps the reference's sun. Two details are not incidental: it writes **absolute** reaches rather than multiplying in place, so applying the layer twice is a no-op instead of compounding to 1.5625× (asserted, down to the same instance coming back); and it sets the machine rows and the `PowerTuning` record **together**, because those are two copies of one fact with no guard keeping them in step — the light mask reads `MachineSpec.LightRadiusTiles`, the opening advice quotes `Power.LampRadiusTiles` in words, and the authored kerb streetlights have no machine row at all and read `Power.StreetLightRadiusTiles`. Had only the machine rows moved, the game would have lit five tiles while the tutorial said four. *Checks:* offline sim 938 passed, 1 skipped (was 936+1); EditMode 1035/1035 (was 1032) — EditMode runs the sim tests too, so both totals move by the same three. PlayMode was **not run**: nothing here touches a scene, a controller or `HudController.Paint`; its three known failures are untouched and still open (`DroppedCargoPlayTests` is REL-125). Three new tests: `ThePortsLightsReachAQuarterFurtherThanTheReferences` states the ratios against `CatalogueData.Build()`'s own rows rather than repeating the numbers, plus the machine/tuning agreement and idempotence; `ALampNowLightsTheTileTheReferenceLeftDark` checks the reach on the ground and that the bigger radius still has an **edge**; and `EveryDataPathTheGameLoadsGivesAPlacedLightItsQuarterMoreReach` checks the same through the asset-backed `Build()` and `BuildLegacy()`, and that `BuildOriginal()` still carries the reference's 4 — that last assertion is the guard that fails the day someone “fixes” this by editing the asset. Two existing tests were adapted to the intended change, not rewritten to pass: one read the constant 7 where it should have read the data's own street-light radius, and one named a tile 5.0 from a lamp to stand for “inside the ring but dark”, which the fifth tile lit — moved to a tile 6.71 out, same claim. *Stated limits:* the performance cost is **unmeasured** — +25% radius is about +56% lit area per light, and the R2 lighting prototype that measured 200 lights at 4/6/12 is deliberately NOT retuned, because it exists to reproduce a recorded measurement; so the port now draws larger lights than any measurement on file covers. Legacy saves get the bigger lights too, since `BuildLegacy()` also applies `DarkWorld`. No save-schema change, no new decision (U-D-71 already holds the owner's four answers). PLAY-06, walls blocking the torch, is REL-131 and is not in this commit. Nobody has played this build. [Evidence](evidence/rel-126/README.md). Next free ids: U-D-72, U-P-30, E-25, F1-39.
- **2026-09-23 — REL-128 (PLAY-03): a pole carries half again as far.** The third of the owner's ten notes from their 2026-09-22 play, verbatim: *“Power pole connection range needs to be increased by about 50%”*. **Pole 8 → 12 tiles, Big pole 12 → 18, Substation 8 → 12** (U-P-30). The owner named the figure (“about 50%”) and one machine (“power pole”); **the substation is the implementer's extension**, stated before building, and it is not tidiness: the authored city's substation lots are reach nodes in their own right with no machine row at all (`PowerGrid.SiteReach`), and the opening's advice sentence describes pole-to-pole linking while quoting a reach in words — it quoted the *substation site's*, which read correctly only because the two numbers happened to match at 8. Raise the pole alone and the tutorial says eight tiles while poles link at twelve. That sentence now quotes the Pole's own reach through a new `PowerGrid.ReachOf(GameData, string kind)` overload, which is what it was always describing and also makes dropping the substation back to 8 a one-line change that breaks nothing. **The issue's own text proposed “Data only — `Tuning - Power` and the generated asset”; that premise is wrong**, for exactly the REL-126 reason: `CatalogueData.g.cs` is generated from `packages/`, which this project may not edit, and is what every offline sim test reads, while the `.asset` is what the game reads — editing only the asset would have left the tests measuring 8 and the game playing 12. So the change is a new overlay layer, `Sim/Data/GridBalance.cs`, applied in **both** chains (`ReferenceData.Create` and `GameDataRegistry.Build`). It is its own layer rather than a line in `DarkWorld` because reach is not light, and because `DarkWorld` is in the `BuildLegacy()` chain where reach must not be: **`BuildLegacy()` deliberately does not apply it** — that path means “the original balance, but still no sun”, reach *is* balance, and a save laid out under eight-tile poles keeps the grid it was built for. It writes absolute reaches rather than a multiplier (a ×1.5 applied in place would compound to 2.25), and sets the machine rows and the `PowerTuning` record together, since the lots read the record and the advice quotes it in words. A kind the layer has no number for keeps its own reach. *Checks:* offline sim **940 passed, 1 skipped** (was 938/1) and Unity EditMode **1038/1038** (was 1035/1035) — EditMode runs the sim tests too, so both totals move by the same three new tests. PlayMode was not run (nothing here touches a scene or a controller); its three known failures stand. **Seven fixtures were re-spaced**, each keeping its claim and its assertions word for word and moving only coordinates — adapted to the intended change, not rewritten to pass: the boundary pair 18/19 became 22/23; a far foundry moved 30 → 36 because an 18-tile big pole covered it from the yard's own; two substation fixtures moved west/east because the lot's own longer reach found what they needed it not to reach; and three light fixtures were re-laid because a lamp's pole at (32,40) is now within reach of a turret's at (32,30), which merged the two circuits a brownout test and a refuelling test each need kept apart. The tightest new clearance is 12.5 tiles against a reach of 12; most are ≥ 13.5. Three neighbouring tests were checked by hand and still pass **and** still mean what they say. *Three tests added:* the three ratios against the generated catalogue's own rows plus idempotence; every other machine's reach unchanged; and, through the asset-backed paths, that `BuildOriginal()` still carries 8/12/8, `Build()` gives 12/18/12, `BuildLegacy()` stays at 8, and `ReferenceData.Create()` and `registry.Build()` answer the same number — the guard that fails the day someone “fixes” this by editing the asset. *Stated limits:* the authored city was **not re-surveyed** — a longer reach can only add links, never remove one, so nothing connected can have come apart, but whether any yard now joins a circuit its designer meant to keep separate is unmeasured (`RealCityRingTests` and the city validators pass unchanged); a longer reach is also a cheaper one, since a Pole costs what it cost and the opening's pole budget was not re-costed. No save-schema change, no new decision (U-D-71 already holds the owner's note and flags the substation as the implementer's). Nobody has played this build. [Evidence](evidence/rel-128/README.md). Next free ids: U-D-72, U-P-31, E-25, F1-39.
- **2026-09-23 — REL-129 (PLAY-04): the resupply card gets out of the way.** The fourth of the owner's ten notes from their 2026-09-22 play, verbatim: *“‘Automatic resupply working’ sticks for a minute after completing before changing to ‘Expand your defences’”*. **`SupplyAckS` 45 → 10 s** (U-P-31); the “Attack repelled” card beside it (`AckS`, 60 s) is untouched. **This is not a defect**, and the entry says so plainly because the note reads like a bug report: the card is meant to hold, it is the acknowledgement that the objective was met, and the mechanism has been deliberate since the opening tutorial was written — only the figure was wrong. Only one of the two cards moved, and the difference is the whole argument: “Attack repelled” reports an event with nothing queued behind it, so 60 s costs the player nothing, while this one stands *between* a finished objective and the next, so every second of it is a second the player has finished the task and cannot see what to do next — which is exactly what the note describes, not “this card is too long” but “it sticks *before changing to* Expand your defences”. What the card advises (watch the Assembler's inputs) is neither time-critical nor only available there. No new layer was needed: `Sim/Data/OpeningBalance.cs` already owns the opening's costs and recipes, is already applied in both data paths and is already absent from `BuildLegacy()`, which is right here too. It writes the value absolutely, so applying the layer twice lands where applying it once did, and data carrying no opening record is left alone. The `.asset` and the generated catalogue are unchanged, for the REL-126/REL-128 reason. *Checks:* offline sim **941 passed, 1 skipped** (was 940/1) and Unity EditMode **1040/1040** (was 1038/1038) — both move by the same two new tests. PlayMode was not run (the card is text from `OpeningQueries.Objective`; no scene or controller changed); its three known failures stand. **No existing test needed changing** — the one objective-chain test that passes through this card advances the clock by 1000 s, far past any window, so it never depended on the figure. *Two tests added:* the card is up on delivery, still up a second before the window closes and replaced by “Expand your defences” a second after — reading the window from `OpeningBalance.SupplyAckSeconds` so the test states the rule rather than a second copy of the number, and pinning only the literal the owner cares about, that the hold is well under the minute they complained about; and the same through the asset-backed path, with `BuildOriginal()` still carrying the reference's 45 and `ReferenceData.Create()` and `registry.Build()` answering one number. *Stated limits:* **nobody has read the card at 10 s** — whether ten seconds is long enough to finish two sentences of advice is a question only a player can answer, and if it proves short the figure is one constant; the card still yields on a timer, not on an action, since making it dismissable is a mechanism change and the note asked for a duration. No save-schema change (the hold is tuning, not state), no new decision. Nobody has played this build. [Evidence](evidence/rel-129/README.md). Next free ids: U-D-72, U-P-32, E-25, F1-39.
- **2026-09-23 — REL-135 (PLAY-10): typing a save name no longer works the hotkeys underneath.** The tenth of the owner's ten notes from their 2026-09-22 play, verbatim: *“When typing in the save menu, if I press ‘B’ it still opens up the build menu. It shouldn’t”*. One method changed, `UiShell.TextInputFocused()`. **The issue's own diagnosis named two stacked faults; only the first is real, and checking that changed the fix.** Real: the method opened `if (_active == null) return false;` — `_active` is the shell's own open drawer, `Rebuild()` collects only elements carrying the `panel` class in the shell's document, and `PauseMenuController` owns its own `UIDocument` and never registers there, so with the save dialog up the shell answered “nobody is typing” before looking at anything. Wrong: that `document.rootVisualElement.focusController` is the shell's *own* controller and cannot see the pause menu's field — **both documents point at one `PanelSettings` asset** (guid `35808e5a…`, `GameUI.unity` at sortingOrder 0 and 10, and `MainMenu.unity`), and documents sharing one PanelSettings are siblings inside one runtime panel with one `FocusController`. Had that claim been true the fix would have been a cross-document focus search every frame; instead the test states the dependency (`Is.SameAs` on the two controllers) so a future screen with its own PanelSettings fails by name, not by symptom. **The visibility walk added alongside it is not tidying:** the pause menu is dismissed by switching a parent to `display: none` over a field that still holds focus (`ShowOverlay` toggles `hidden`; `PauseMenu.uss:24`), and UI Toolkit does not reliably blur an element switched off that way — trusting focus alone would have traded this bug for a worse one, B dead for the rest of the session after one save name. **Three keys move with B**, since the same method gates Tab here and is published as `WorldInput.UiTextInputFocused`, which gates the digit slot keys and R. **Two screens the issue also listed needed nothing:** the settings key-binding capture is a `TextField` in `GameUI.unity` beside the shell and is fixed by this same commit; the title screen's seed field is in `MainMenu.unity`, loaded `Single` with no `UiShell` and no `WorldInput` in it, so there are no hotkeys there to fire — satisfied by construction, not by this change, and said so plainly so nobody reads a fix into it. *One test added:* it pauses, clicks Save through a real submit event, focuses the real `save-name` field, presses B on a real virtual keyboard and asserts no panel opened; then unpauses — leaving the field focused but hidden — and asserts B works again. **It was proved to bite**: with the old guard put back for one run it failed with *“B typed into a save name opened the build menu underneath it. Expected: null, But was: \"build-panel\"”*, the owner's note reproduced exactly; the temporary line was then removed and the editor recompiled clean. A test that focused a field in the shell's *own* drawer would have passed throughout the bug's whole life, which is why this one is driven through the authored scenes and real devices. *Checks:* Unity EditMode **1040/1040** (unchanged — no EditMode test covers runtime focus) and Unity PlayMode in full, **98 total: 93 passed, 0 failed, 5 skipped**. The offline sim suite was not run (nothing in `Sim` changed). **The three known PlayMode failures now pass, and this commit is not claimed as the fix** — the last full measurement was at `d3827d18` and REL-14, REL-62, REL-66, REL-126, REL-128 and REL-129 have landed since; no bisect was run. REL-125 was re-checked alone, since its title is “fails when run on its own”, and passed 1/1; one green run does not retire an intermittent failure, so it stays open with the measurement recorded on it. The five skips are all declared and environmental. No number was invented, so no U-P row; no new decision; no save-schema change. Nobody has played this build. [Evidence](evidence/rel-135/README.md). Next free ids: U-D-72, U-P-32, E-25, F1-39.
- **2026-09-23 — REL-127 (PLAY-02): buildings give off a small light of their own.** The second of the owner's ten notes from their 2026-09-22 play, verbatim: *“Buildings should have their own light, not large, but big enough to illuminate a small area around them, excludes walls and power poles.”* Asked whether that light should be real or only seen, they chose **seen only** (U-D-71 b) — so the glow is drawn by `LightingPresenter` and **nothing in `Sim` may read it**, which `GlowStaysDrawnTests` now enforces by source scan: `MachineSpec.LightRadiusTiles` is what `LightSources.Collect` reads, and raiders hesitate on lit ground, so a real glow on every building would have made raids easier without anyone deciding to. **Reach 2.5 tiles beyond the footprint, peak 0.75, measured from the edge so a 6×6 Depot and a 2×2 chest spill the same** (U-P-32); *the owner named no figure — both numbers are the implementer's under U-D-28*. **The issue's own acceptance line was too literal**: “every buildable except wall, gate and the two poles” would have made belts, undergrounds, splitters, inserters and barricades glow, and forty glowing belt tiles is exactly the “large” light the note rules out — so the rule reads the catalogue (footprint ≥ 2, no power reach, not already a light) and lands on thirteen buildings, which also excludes REL-132's one-tile Gate with no special case. `LightingPresenter.Reveal` folds the glow in beside the torch beam, because its one consumer (`EnemyPresenter`) would otherwise paint a flat silhouette over a raider standing on visibly lit ground. New: `Sim/UI/BuildingGlow.cs`, 8 sim tests, 1 Play test, 1 editor scan test. Offline sim **949 passed, 1 skipped** (was 941/1); Unity EditMode **1049/1049** (was 1040/1040); Unity PlayMode **99 total, 94 passed, 0 failed, 5 skipped** (was 98/93/0/5) — the new Play test ran rather than skipping. The new tests were shown to bite: mutating the rule turned 949 passed/0 failed into 946/3, and restoring it returned 949/0. Evidence `Unity/Docs/evidence/rel-127/README.md`. **Not seen in play** — whether 2.5 tiles at 0.75 reads as “not large” is a question only the owner can answer.
- **2026-09-23 — REL-130 (PLAY-05): the mouse wheel opens the view by half again.** The fifth of the owner's ten notes from their 2026-09-22 play, verbatim: *“Can we have a zoom out functionality, mouse scroll. Not too far, just about another 50%”*. There was no zoom at all before this — `CameraRig.Awake` set `orthographicSize` once and nothing ever wrote it again. **`viewTilesHigh` (20 in the scene) stops being *the* view and becomes the CLOSEST view**; the wheel opens outward to 30 tiles high in four notches and back, gliding at 16 per second (U-P-33). *The owner gave the 50%; the four notches and the 16/s are the implementer's under U-D-28.* The factor multiplies the **authored** `viewTilesHigh` rather than being a second authored number, so moving the opening framing moves the zoom-out with it and the two cannot drift apart — the test asserts that relationship, not the literal 30. **There is deliberately no zoom in:** wheeling up returns to the opening framing and finds nothing past it, because letting the player go closer than the authored framing changes how the game reads at rest and is a different decision from the one the note makes. **It is not saved** — zoom is a view preference like a window size, so no `SaveSchema` change and a load re-opens at the closest framing. **No action was added to `RelightControls.inputactions`**: that file carries the player's saved rebindings through `Bindings.cs`, and `MouseFlashlightPresenter` already sets the precedent of reading `Mouse.current` directly, so a map entry for something nobody rebinds would disturb them for no gain. The wheel is guarded by `WorldInput.UiPointerProbe`, the same probe that decides whether a click belongs to the world, so a drawer's list scrolls instead of the city zooming. Two orderings are load-bearing and are commented as such: `Zoom()` runs as the **first** line of `LateUpdate` so the map clamp below is computed against the size the frame will actually draw (running it after would clamp a frame behind and show a sliver of black at an edge on the frame the view widens), and `orthographicSize` is written **only when it differs**, because `CameraInsetTests` and the opening UI tests assert exact float equality of that field across an open/close cycle (D-UI-10). `LightingPresenter` needed no change: `Rect` reads `orthographicSize` live each frame and pads three tiles, so the darkness resizes with the view on its own. *Checks:* Unity PlayMode in full, **105 total: 100 passed, 0 failed, 5 skipped** (was 99/94/0/5) — the six new tests all ran. EditMode and the offline sim suite were not run: nothing outside `Presentation` and one Play test changed, and neither suite loads a scene camera. *Six Play tests added,* driven in the authored city rather than on a bare GameObject because both things that could go wrong are scene-shaped — the map clamp has to hold at the wider view on the real 864×576 city, and the darkness has to keep covering the screen; a rig built in a test would have neither. Four drive `Wheel(float)`, the seam where the range and the clamp live, so the clamping is testable on a machine with no mouse; the other two turn a **real** virtual mouse wheel, because the first four would pass just as happily if nobody had wired the mouse to the rig. **The tests were shown to bite:** dropping the `Mathf.Clamp` and the `UiPointerProbe` guard together turned 6 passed into 4 passed / 2 failed — *“wheeling out past the end opened the view further than half again. Expected: 30.0f, But was: 37.5f”* and *“a wheel turned over an open panel zoomed the city behind it. Expected: 20.0f, But was: 22.5f”* — and restoring both returned 6/6. **One test first written wrong, and fixed rather than left:** the panel-guard test aimed at the middle of the screen, the status drawer does not cover it, so on the first full run the test skipped itself and proved nothing; it now works the drawer's own `worldBound` centre back into screen coordinates through a two-corner probe of the panel mapping, and it is the test that caught the missing guard above. *Stated limits:* **not seen in play** — whether four notches at 16/s feels right on a real wheel, and whether 30 tiles is the owner's “not too far”, are questions only they can answer; the wheel is not rebindable, and a trackpad's small deltas are treated as notches by sign, which has not been tried on a trackpad. No save-schema change, no new decision (U-D-71 already holds the owner's note). Nobody has played this build. [Evidence](evidence/rel-130/README.md). Next free ids: U-D-72, U-P-34, E-25, F1-39.
- **2026-09-23 — REL-131 (PLAY-06): walls stop the torch.** The sixth of the owner's ten notes from their 2026-09-22 play, verbatim: *“Existing and placed walls should block light from the player torch, but not light from placed objects”*. **The premise was checked before anything was built, and half the note asked for nothing:** light from placed objects has been blocked by walls since REL-116 / L-02, so only the torch was going through walls. Shown that, the owner chose **“only stop the torch”** — not the recommendation — so **REL-116 stands and placed lamps are untouched**; `LightBlockingTests` passes unchanged, which is that acceptance item. **The torch cannot be expressed as a `Light`** — `Light.Dir` is one of eight fixed directions and the torch points wherever the mouse is, a continuous angle — so `StampWindow`'s shadow was lifted out into a new `LightRules.Shadow(...)` that takes the shape from its caller: the same `Opaque` rule, the same segment walk, the same both-end-tiles exemption. A second shadow rule written for the torch alone would have let the torch and the lamp standing beside it disagree about the same wall, and `ItAgreesWithTheStampALampGetsThroughTheSameWall` puts both questions to one wall and requires the same answer tile for tile. **The caller marks what it wants tested** (1 = ask, 0 = do not ask), so a 60° cone pays for the ~150 tiles it reaches rather than the 900 in its bounding box, and tiles nobody asked about are left alone instead of coming back as shadow. **Still picture only:** `LightQueries.LitAt` has never known the torch exists and does not learn it here — the mask does not move, `Builds` does not tick and `st.Rev` does not change, all asserted. Two details are deliberate: a tile is marked when the cone touches its centre **or any of its four corners**, because the overlay is supersampled and a texel near a tile's edge can be inside the cone when the tile's centre is not, and an untested tile reads as shadow, which would bite a notch out of the cone's rim; and the shadow is built **above every early return** in `LateUpdate`, because `EnemyPresenter` reads `Reveal` on frames the overlay draws nothing and a stale box would shadow a beam that has since moved. The glow at one's own feet (`GlowTiles` = 1.75) is **not** occluded — `Beam()` returns it before consulting the shadow, so standing with your nose to a wall does not put you in the dark. `Shadow` returns false without walking a single line when its box holds no occluder, which is most of the map most of the time. *Checks:* offline sim **957 passed, 1 skipped** (was 949/1), Unity EditMode **1057/1057** (was 1049/1049) — both move by the same 8 new sim tests — and Unity PlayMode in full, **107 total: 102 passed, 0 failed, 5 skipped** (was 105/100/0/5); the five skips are the declared environmental ones. *Ten tests added:* 8 in the sim suite on the shared rule, including the lamp-agreement test above and a cost test that holds one 30×30 shadow well inside a 16 ms frame on the full city with a building on every block; and 2 in the authored scene, that the cone actually stops at a facade while the facade's own face stays lit, and that the feet glow survives a wall pressed against you. **Both halves were shown to bite:** stopping `Shadow` clearing what the line test rejects turned 8 passed into 4 passed / 4 failed — and the four that still passed are exactly the four that assert no occlusion — while removing the gate from `Beam()` turned the 2 Play tests into 0 passed / 2 failed with their own messages; restoring each returned 8/8 and 2/2. That second mutation is the one that matters, because a shadow rule that works perfectly and that nothing consults would have passed every sim test in the suite. *Stated limits:* the PlayMode tests use an **authored** building rather than a placed wall, so they need no inventory and cannot skip themselves — `st.Walls` is covered from the sim side, where both occluders run through the one `Opaque` rule; and the per-frame cost is measured on a synthetic full-city map, not profiled in a running game. No number was invented, so no U-P row; no new decision (U-D-71 already holds the owner's note and their “only stop the torch” answer); no save-schema change. Nobody has played this build. [Evidence](evidence/rel-131/README.md). Next free ids: U-D-72, U-P-34, E-25, F1-39.
- **2026-09-23 — REL-133 (PLAY-08): a building shows that it is being repaired.** The eighth of the owner's ten notes from their 2026-09-22 play, verbatim: *“When a building is being repaired, it needs a little animation or something to show it's being repaired apart from just text”*. **The premise was checked first and held exactly**: a running repair was announced in `HudController`, `StatusPanelViewModel` and the workshop's machine repair card, all three in words, and **nothing in `Presentation/` drew anything on the building** — there was no half-built version of this to extend. Two new files: `Sim/UI/BuildingCondition.cs` answers which building is being repaired and how far through it is, and `Presentation/Building/BuildingConditionVisual.cs` draws a progress ring with a welding arc at its leading end and four falling sparks (U-P-34); `MachineView` and `MachinePresenter` gained one gate each. **The rule sits in `Sim/UI` beside `DarknessLook`, `LightSweep` and `BuildingGlow`** — in the Sim assembly so it is testable offline, in `UI` so the simulation may not read it. It is deliberately **not** folded into `HomeQueries`: the two things there that touch repair do far more work than a picture should pay for every frame (`MachineRepairCard` prices the repair, `RepairProblem` walks the engineer's reach and pockets), while these three answers are field reads. **The total is read from the same two tuning rows `HomeCommands` reads, not from `HomeCore.Price`** — `Price` re-reads hit points, so as a denominator it would disagree with the countdown the instant a repair healed the core past the recommission threshold and the ring would jump. **It is not part of `MachineActivityVisual`, and that is the load-bearing decision**: that class's `Supports` gate is belts, arms, power, processors, miners, turrets and wrecks — a Wall, a chest and a pole are outside it, and they are exactly the things that get repaired. Widening the gate would also have stamped the production operating badge on every damaged wall. A second, independent visual keeps the existing `isVisible` culling and per-view pooling for free. **The Home core is drawn too, from the presenter**, because it is a rect on `HomeState` and not a `Machine` — it has no `MachineView`, and nothing in `Presentation/` drew it at all before this. It is a building to the player, the one they repair most, and the note carves nothing out. Its object's position is written every frame rather than once (`HomeCore.Ensure` can place the core on a later tick and a loaded save brings one that may stand elsewhere), and `MachinePresenter.Clear()` destroys only `_views`, so the object survives a session change while `AnimateCore` calls `Hide()` on the first frame after a load. **Everything is drawn inside the footprint, centred, leaving the space above the top edge clear for REL-134's health bar** — a building being repaired is always a damaged building, so the two are on screen together nearly every time and cannot collide by construction. Strokes sort at `DrawOrder.MachineCue` (512), above `Darkness` (500), so a repair is readable in the dark, which is when repairs happen. *Checks:* offline sim **968 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** (was 957/1/0), Unity EditMode **1068/1068** (was 1057/1057) and Unity PlayMode in full, **109 total: 104 passed, 0 failed, 5 skipped** (was 107 total: 102 passed, 0 failed, 5 skipped). The five skips are the declared environmental ones and are unchanged. *Thirteen tests added.* The eleven sim tests run the **real** `RepairCommand` and the real `HomeCorePhase` rather than setting `st.Home`'s fields by hand, because a test that wrote `RepairKind` itself would still pass if the command stopped setting it; cancelling, finishing and going down (REL-16) each go through their own real path for the same reason. Two of them guard things easy to get wrong: the core's `RepairId` sentinel is `-1`, so the rule is asked about `-1` and must answer **false** for a machine; and an old save can hand the presenter a `RepairRemaining` longer than the total, which must clamp rather than draw the ring the wrong way round. The two Play tests count the **enabled strokes** under the building and not only the flag, and use a **chest** on purpose — it is outside `MachineActivityVisual.Supports`, so it is one of the buildings the existing activity strokes never touch. **All three halves were shown to bite:** dropping the `RepairId` check took the sim class to 10/1 (`StartingAMachineRepairMarksThatMachineAndOnlyThatMachine`); always measuring against `RepairSeconds` took it to 10/1 (`ARecommissionIsMeasuredAgainstTheRecommissionTimeNotTheRepairTime`); removing the drawing from **both** `MachineView.Animate` and `MachinePresenter.LateUpdate` took the Play tests to **0 passed / 2 failed** with their own messages — *“a repair is running on this chest and the building is not showing it”* and *“the core is being repaired and nothing is showing it”*. All three restored to green. The third is the one that matters: without it, a rule that answers perfectly and that nothing draws would have passed all eleven sim tests. *Stated limits:* **no simulation change** — no new sim state, no new tick work, no save-schema change, asserted by `AskingChangesNothingInTheSimulation` over 200 calls (including `st.Turrets.Units.Count`, so `Find` cannot quietly become `Of` and start creating a damage entry just by being asked). The inspect card's wording is untouched; the note asks for something *on the building*. **Not seen in play** — whether a ring-and-arc reads as “being repaired” at a glance, and whether 0.24 tiles is big enough to notice on a Wall, are questions only the owner can answer. Nobody has played this build. [Evidence](evidence/rel-133/README.md). Next free ids: U-D-72, U-P-35, E-25, F1-39.
- **2026-09-23 — REL-134 (PLAY-09): a damaged building carries a health bar.** The ninth of the owner's ten notes from their 2026-09-22 play, verbatim: *“When a building has lost health, it should show a small health bar above it to show it's been damaged. Also when inspecting a building, the health bar should display more prominently”*. **The premise was checked first and held exactly:** `grep -rn "health-bar\|HpBar\|healthBar\|hp-bar"` across every `.cs`, `.uxml` and `.uss` returned **nothing** — hit points were reported in words and only in words (`HomeQueries.MachineRepairLine` on the inspect card, the HUD's core row, and `StatusPanelViewModel`, whose HP is the **engineer's**, not a building's). So the “more prominently” half is a row that had *no* bar at all rather than a small one; both halves are new. **Seven pieces:** the rule `Sim/UI/BuildingCondition.Health`/`CoreHealth` with the `HealthReading` struct; the drawing `Presentation/Building/BuildingHealthVisual`; a gate in `MachineView.Animate` ahead of REL-133's repair gate; the core's in `MachinePresenter.AnimateCore` with a lazy `HalfView()`; and the card's `machine-health` track, fill and figure in `InventoryPanel.uxml`/`.uss`/`Controller`. **The rule reads `TurretRules` directly, not `MachineRepairCard`, and the chain was followed to the bottom rather than assumed:** `MachineRepairCard` → `HomeCore.Price` → `HomeCore.MachineDefenceHp`, which *is* `TurretRules.MaxHp` and `TurretRules.Hp` — everything the card adds on top (pricing, reach, pockets, a refusal string) is work a picture drawn every frame should not pay for. `AssertAgreesWithTheCard` puts the two side by side in four damage states plus the core and requires them equal within 1e-9, so the claim is tested rather than asserted in a comment. **The bands live on the rule** (`HurtBelow = 0.66`, `CriticalBelow = 0.30`, `public const`) because the world bar is drawn in `Presentation` and the card's is styled in `UI`, in different assemblies, and two copies of 0.66 drift without anyone noticing. **Thickness follows the camera and width does not:** REL-130 opened the view by half again, so thickness is `orthographicSize × 0.016` clamped — the same pixels at either notch — while width stays in world units and tracks the footprint (0.9–2.4 tiles), which is what keeps the bar attached to its building; the wheel is driven for real in `TheBarKeepsItsSizeOnScreenWhenTheWheelOpensTheView`. The bar sits **above** the footprint, in the space `BuildingConditionVisual` deliberately leaves clear, so REL-133's ring and this cannot collide by construction. *Numbers (U-P-35):* the note names none, so all are the implementer's under U-D-28. *Checks:* offline sim **979 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** (was 968/1/0), Unity EditMode **1079/1079** (was 1068/1068), Unity PlayMode **113 total — 105 passed, 3 failed, 5 skipped**. **The three failures are the known intermittent trio and a bisect that said otherwise was wrong — recorded, not deleted.** They are `DroppedCargoPlayTests` (REL-125), `OpeningUiPlayTests` U6 and `Ui.EscapeCancelsRepairPlayTests`: the same three by name that REL-135 logged as “the three known PlayMode failures”, 3 failed at `d3827d18`, 0 at REL-135. They were first read as a regression from this change and bisected — but every step was a **single run of a test known to flake**, which is a coin landing twice, not a cause. Re-run alone with this change fully in place: `OpeningUiPlayTests` **4/4**, `DroppedCargoPlayTests` **1/1**, `EscapeCancelsRepairPlayTests` **2/2**. There is no mechanism either — all three are a key press that did not take, this change touches no input path, and `PaintHealthBar` is reached only through `PaintMachineControls`, which returns at `if (m == null)` when no machine is paired, as in U6's core. **This commit is not claimed as their fix; REL-125 stays open.** *Both halves were shown to bite:* four mutations, the decisive one removing the drawing from both wirings and taking the 4 Play tests to **0 passed / 4 failed** — without it a rule that reads perfectly and that nothing draws would have passed all eleven sim tests. **One mutation was a no-op and is reported as such** (dropping the `max > 0` guard in `Health` left 11/11, because for an undamageable building `TurretRules.Hp` returns 0 and the two readings are observationally identical); the sharper `Exists => Max >= 0` mutation is the one that catches that case. *Stated limits:* **no simulation change** — no new sim state, no new tick work, `SaveSchema.Version` stays 12, asserted by `AskingChangesNothingInTheSimulation` over 200 calls. The HUD and the status panel are untouched. **Not seen in play** — whether 0.16 tiles reads as “a small health bar” over a Wall, and whether the card's bar is prominent enough, are questions only the owner can answer. Nobody has played this build. [Evidence](evidence/rel-134/README.md). Next free ids: U-D-72, U-P-36, E-25, F1-39.
